href="/w/index.php?title=Francesco_Rossi_(bishop)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Francesco Rossi (bishop) (page does not exist)">Francesco Rossi</a></th><td class="infobox-data">26 May 1963</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Igino_Eugenio_Cardinale" title="Igino Eugenio Cardinale">Igino Eugenio Cardinale</a></th><td class="infobox-data">20 October 1963</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Albert_Reuben_Edward_Thomas" title="Albert Reuben Edward Thomas">Albert Reuben Edward Thomas</a></th><td class="infobox-data">20 October 1963</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/w/index.php?title=Giovanni_Fallani&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Giovanni Fallani (page does not Gerardus Maria Willebrands</a></th><td class="infobox-data">28 June 1964</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/w/index.php?title=Leobard_D%27Souza&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Leobard D&#39;Souza (page does not exist)">Leobard D'Souza</a></th><td class="infobox-data">3 December 1964</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Giacomo_Violardo" title="Giacomo Violardo">Giacomo Violardo</a></th><td class="infobox-data">19 March 1966</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a July 1967</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Ernesto_Civardi" title="Ernesto Civardi">Ernesto Civardi</a></th><td class="infobox-data">16 July 1967</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Paul_Marcinkus" title="Paul Marcinkus">Paul Casimir Marcinkus</a></th><td class="infobox-data">6 January 1969</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Louis_Vangeke" title="Louis Vangeke">Louis Vangeke</a></th><td class="infobox-data">3 December 1970</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Annibale_Bugnini" title="Annibale Bugnini">Annibale Bugnini</a></th><td class="infobox-data">13 February 1972</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Casoria" title="Giuseppe Casoria">Giuseppe Casoria</a></th><td class="infobox-data">13 February 1972</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/w/index.php?title=Enrico_Bartolucci_Panaroni&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Enrico Bartolucci Panaroni (page does not exist)">Enrico Bartolucci Panaroni</a></th><td class="infobox-data">29 June 1973</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Jean_Jerome_Hamer" class="mw-redirect" title="Jean Jerome Hamer">Jean Jerome Hamer</a></th><td class="infobox-data">29 June 1973</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Andrzej_Maria_Deskur" title="Andrzej Maria Deskur">Andrzej Maria Deskur</a></th><td class="infobox-data">30 June 1974</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Nicola_Rotunno" title="Nicola Rotunno">Nicola Rotunno</a></th><td class="infobox-data">30 June 1974</td></tr></tbody></table></td></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below"><a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="Pope Paul (disambiguation)">Other popes  named Paul</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pope Paul VI</b> (<a href="/wiki/Latin_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin language">Latin</a>: <i lang="la">Paulus VI</i>; <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Paolo VI</i>; born <b>Giovanni Battista Enrico Antonio Maria Montini</b>, <small>Italian:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="it-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Italian" title="Help:IPA/Italian">[dʒoˈvanni batˈtista enˈriːko anˈtɔːnjo maˈriːa monˈtiːni]</a></span>; 26 September 1897&#160;&#8211;&#32;6 August 1978) was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> and sovereign of the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City State</a> from 21 June 1963 to his death in August 1978. Succeeding <a href="/wiki/John_XXIII" class="mw-redirect" title="John XXIII">John XXIII</a>, he continued the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a>, which he closed in 1965, implementing its numerous reforms. He fostered improved ecumenical relations with <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox" class="mw-redirect" title="Eastern Orthodox">Eastern Orthodox</a> and <a href="/wiki/Protestant_churches" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant churches">Protestant churches</a>, which resulted in many historic meetings and agreements.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> In January 1964, he flew to the <a href="/wiki/Hashemite_Kingdom_of_Jordan" class="mw-redirect" title="Hashemite Kingdom of Jordan">Hashemite Kingdom of Jordan</a>. This was the first time a reigning pontiff had flown on an airplane,<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> the first papal pilgrimage to the Holy Land,<sup id="cite_ref-HL_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HL-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> and the first time a Pope had left Italy in more than a century.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Montini served in the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See%27s_Secretariat_of_State" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy See&#39;s Secretariat of State">Holy See's Secretariat of State</a> from 1922 to 1954. While in the Secretariat of State, Montini and <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a> were considered to be the closest and most influential advisors of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a>. In 1954, Pius named Montini <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Milan">Archbishop of Milan</a>, the largest Italian diocese. Montini later became the Secretary of the Italian Bishops' Conference. John XXIII elevated him to the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a> in 1958, and after the death of John XXIII, Montini was considered one of his most likely successors.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993322–23_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993322–23-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Upon his election to the papacy, Montini took the name Paul VI.
</p><p>He re-convened the Second Vatican Council, which had automatically closed with the death of John XXIII. After the council had concluded its work, Paul VI took charge of the interpretation and implementation of its mandates, often walking a thin line between the conflicting expectations of various groups within Catholicism. The magnitude and depth of the reforms affecting all fields of church life during his pontificate exceeded similar reform programmes of his predecessors and successors. Paul VI spoke repeatedly to Marian conventions and <a href="/wiki/Mariology_of_the_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Mariology of the Catholic Church">Mariological</a> meetings, visited Marian shrines and issued three <a href="/wiki/Mariology_of_Pope_Paul_VI" class="mw-redirect" title="Mariology of Pope Paul VI">Marian encyclicals</a>. Following <a href="/wiki/Ambrose_of_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="Ambrose of Milan">Ambrose of Milan</a>, he named Mary as the <a href="/wiki/Mother_of_the_Church" title="Mother of the Church">Mother of the Church</a> during the Second Vatican Council.<sup id="cite_ref-InterCom_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-InterCom-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Paul VI described himself as a humble servant for a suffering humanity and demanded significant changes from the rich in North America and Europe in favour of the poor in the Third World.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> His positions on <a href="/wiki/Birth_control" title="Birth control">birth control</a>, promulgated famously in the 1968 encyclical <span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la"><a href="/wiki/Humanae_vitae" title="Humanae vitae">Humanae vitae</a></i></span>, were often contested, especially in Western Europe and North America. The same opposition emerged in reaction to the political aspects of some of his teaching.
</p><p>Following the standard procedures that lead to sainthood, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> declared that the late pontiff had lived a life of <a href="/wiki/Heroic_virtue" title="Heroic virtue">heroic virtue</a> and conferred the title of <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a> upon him on 20 December 2012. <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> beatified him on 19 October 2014 after the recognition of a miracle attributed to his intercession. His liturgical feast was celebrated on the date of his birth on 26 September until 2019 when it was changed to the date of his sacerdotal ordination on 29 May.<sup id="cite_ref-feastday_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-feastday-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Pope Francis canonised Paul VI on 14 October 2018.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MontiniMay291920.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/MontiniMay291920.jpg/175px-MontiniMay291920.jpg" decoding="async" width="175" height="213" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/MontiniMay291920.jpg/263px-MontiniMay291920.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/MontiniMay291920.jpg/350px-MontiniMay291920.jpg 2x" data-file-width="890" data-file-height="1082" /></a><figcaption>Montini on the day of his ordination in 1920.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Giovanni Battista Enrico Antonio Maria Montini was born in the village of <a href="/wiki/Concesio" title="Concesio">Concesio</a>, in the <a href="/wiki/Province_of_Brescia" title="Province of Brescia">Province of Brescia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lombardy" title="Lombardy">Lombardy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>, in 1897. His father, Giorgio Montini (1860–1943), was a lawyer, journalist, director of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Action" title="Catholic Action">Catholic Action</a>, and member of the Italian Parliament. His mother, Giudetta Alghisi (1874–1943), was from a family of rural nobility. He had two brothers, Francesco Montini (1900–1971), who became a physician, and Lodovico Montini (1896–1990), who became a lawyer and politician.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196420–21_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196420–21-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> On 30 September 1897, he was baptised with the name Giovanni Battista Enrico Antonio Maria Montini.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196419_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196419-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> He attended the Cesare Arici school, run by the <a href="/wiki/Jesuits" title="Jesuits">Jesuits</a>, and in 1916 received a diploma from the <a href="/wiki/Arnaldo_da_Brescia" class="mw-redirect" title="Arnaldo da Brescia">Arnaldo da Brescia</a> public school in <a href="/wiki/Brescia" title="Brescia">Brescia</a>. His education was often interrupted by bouts of illness.
</p><p>In 1916, he entered the <a href="/wiki/Seminary" title="Seminary">seminary</a> to become a Catholic priest. He was <a href="/wiki/Ordination" title="Ordination">ordained</a> on 29 May 1920 in Brescia and celebrated his first <a href="/wiki/Mass_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Mass in the Catholic Church">Mass</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_delle_Grazie,_Brescia" title="Santa Maria delle Grazie, Brescia">Basilica of Santa Maria delle Grazie</a> in Brescia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196426_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196426-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Montini concluded his studies in <a href="/wiki/Milan" title="Milan">Milan</a> with a doctorate in <a href="/wiki/Canon_law" title="Canon law">canon law</a> in the same year.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988419_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988419-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> He later studied at the <a href="/wiki/Gregorian_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Gregorian University">Gregorian University</a>, the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Rome_La_Sapienza" class="mw-redirect" title="University of Rome La Sapienza">University of Rome La Sapienza</a> and, at the request of <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Pizzardo" title="Giuseppe Pizzardo">Giuseppe Pizzardo</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Accademia_dei_Nobili_Ecclesiastici" class="mw-redirect" title="Accademia dei Nobili Ecclesiastici">Accademia dei Nobili Ecclesiastici</a>. In 1922, at the age of twenty-five, again at the request of Giuseppe Pizzardo, Montini entered the <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_State_(Holy_See)" title="Secretariat of State (Holy See)">Secretariat of State</a>, where he worked under Pizzardo together with <a href="/wiki/Francesco_Borgongini-Duca" class="mw-redirect" title="Francesco Borgongini-Duca">Francesco Borgongini-Duca</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Ottaviani" title="Alfredo Ottaviani">Alfredo Ottaviani</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carlo_Grano" title="Carlo Grano">Carlo Grano</a>, <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a> and <a href="/wiki/Francis_Spellman" title="Francis Spellman">Francis Spellman</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196431_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196431-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Consequently, he never had an appointment as a parish priest. In 1925 he helped found the publishing house Morcelliana in Brescia, focused on promoting a 'Christian-inspired culture'.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Vatican_career">Vatican career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Vatican career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Diplomatic_service">Diplomatic service</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Diplomatic service">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Montini had just one foreign posting in the diplomatic service of the Holy See as Secretary in the office of the papal nuncio to <a href="/wiki/Second_Polish_Republic" title="Second Polish Republic">Poland</a> in 1923. Of the <a href="/wiki/Nationalism" title="Nationalism">nationalism</a> he experienced there he wrote: "This form of nationalism treats foreigners as enemies, especially foreigners with whom one has common frontiers. Then one seeks the expansion of one's own country at the expense of the immediate neighbours. People grow up with a feeling of being hemmed in. Peace becomes a transient compromise between wars."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFappaniMolinariMontini1979404_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFappaniMolinariMontini1979404-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> He described his experience in Warsaw as "useful, though not always joyful".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFappaniMolinariMontini1979265_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFappaniMolinariMontini1979265-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> When he became pope, the Communist government of Poland refused him permission to visit Poland on a Marian pilgrimage.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Roman_Curia">Roman Curia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Roman Curia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_XII_with_Monsignor_Montini.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Pius_XII_with_Monsignor_Montini.jpg/220px-Pius_XII_with_Monsignor_Montini.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="265" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Pius_XII_with_Monsignor_Montini.jpg/330px-Pius_XII_with_Monsignor_Montini.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Pius_XII_with_Monsignor_Montini.jpg/440px-Pius_XII_with_Monsignor_Montini.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1468" data-file-height="1767" /></a><figcaption>Montini photographed alongside <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> during his service at the Secretariat of State</figcaption></figure>
<p>His organisational skills led him to a career in the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a>, the papal civil service. On 19 October 1925, he was appointed a <a href="/wiki/Papal_chamberlain" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal chamberlain">papal chamberlain</a> in the rank of Supernumerary Privy Chamberlain of His Holiness.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In 1931, Pacelli appointed him to teach history at the Pontifical Academy for Diplomats;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988419_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988419-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> he was promoted to Domestic Prelate of His Holiness on 8 July of the same year.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>  On 24 September 1936, he was appointed a Referendary Prelate of the Supreme Tribunal of the Apostolic Signatura.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 16 December 1937,<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> after his mentor Giuseppe Pizzardo was named a cardinal and was succeeded by <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a>, Montini was named Substitute for Ordinary Affairs under Cardinal Pacelli, the Secretary of State. His immediate supervisor was <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a>, with whom he got along well. He was further appointed Consultor of the Supreme Sacred Congregation of the Holy Office and of the Sacred Consistorial Congregation on 24 December,<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> and was promoted to <a href="/wiki/Protonotary_apostolic" title="Protonotary apostolic">Protonotary Apostolic</a> (<i>ad instar participantium</i>), the most senior class of papal prelate, on 10 May 1938.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pacelli became <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> in 1939 and confirmed Montini's appointment as Substitute under the new <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a> <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Maglione" title="Luigi Maglione">Luigi Maglione</a>. In that role, roughly that of a chief of staff, he met the pope every morning until 1954 and developed a rather close relationship with him. Of his service to two popes he wrote:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>It is true, my service to the pope was not limited to the political or extraordinary affairs according to Vatican language. The goodness of Pope Pius XII opened to me the opportunity to look into the thoughts, even into the soul of this great pontiff. I could quote many details how Pius XII, always using measured and moderate speech, was hiding, nay revealing a noble position of great strength and fearless courage.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196458_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196458-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>When war broke out, Maglione, Tardini, and Montini were the principal figures in the <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_State_(Holy_See)" title="Secretariat of State (Holy See)">Secretariat of State</a> of the Holy See.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific page or range of pages in which the material appears. (January 2014)">page&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Montini was in charge of taking care of the "ordinary affairs" of the Secretariat of State, which took much of the mornings of every working day. In the afternoon he moved to the third floor into the Office of the Private Secretary of the Pontiff. Pius XII did not have a personal secretary. As did several popes before him, he delegated the secretarial functions he needed to the <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_State_(Holy_See)" title="Secretariat of State (Holy See)">Secretariat of State</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196457_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196457-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> During the war years, thousands of letters from all parts of the world arrived at the desk of the pope, most of them asking for understanding, prayer, and help. Montini's task was to formulate the replies in the name of Pius XII, expressing his empathy, and understanding and providing help, where possible.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196457_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196457-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the request of the pope, Montini created an information office regarding prisoners of war and refugees, which from 1939 until 1947 received almost ten million requests for information about missing persons and produced over eleven million replies.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPallenberg196071_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPallenberg196071-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Montini was several times attacked by <a href="/wiki/Benito_Mussolini" title="Benito Mussolini">Benito Mussolini</a>'s government for meddling in politics, but the Holy See consistently defended him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993155_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993155-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> When Maglione died in 1944, Pius XII appointed Tardini and Montini together as joint heads of <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_State_(Holy_See)" title="Secretariat of State (Holy See)">Secretariat of State</a>, each with the title of Pro-Secretary of State. Montini's admiration was almost filial when he described Pope Pius XII:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>His richly cultivated mind, his unusual capacity for thought and study led him to avoid all distractions and every unnecessary relaxation. He wished to enter fully into the history of his own afflicted time: with a deep understanding, that he was himself a part of that history. He wished to participate fully in it, to share his sufferings in his own heart and soul.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993195_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993195-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>As Pro-Secretary of State, Montini coordinated the activities of assistance to the persecuted hidden in convents, parishes, seminaries, and in Catholic schools.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
At the request of the pope, Montini established together with <a href="/wiki/Ferdinando_Baldelli" title="Ferdinando Baldelli">Ferdinando Baldelli</a> and <a href="/wiki/Otto_Faller" title="Otto Faller">Otto Faller</a> the <a href="/wiki/Pontificia_Commissione_di_Assistenza" title="Pontificia Commissione di Assistenza">Pontificia Commissione di Assistenza</a> (<i>Pontifical Commission for Assistance</i>), which aided large number of Romans and refugees from everywhere with shelter, food and other material assistance. In Rome alone this organisation distributed almost two million portions of free food in 1944.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPallenberg196072_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPallenberg196072-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> The Papal Residence of <a href="/wiki/Papal_Palace_of_Castel_Gandolfo" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal Palace of Castel Gandolfo">Castel Gandolfo</a> was opened to refugees, as was Vatican City in so far as space allowed. Some 15,000 persons lived in Castel Gandolfo alone, supported by the <a href="/wiki/Pontificia_Commissione_di_Assistenza" title="Pontificia Commissione di Assistenza">Pontificia Commissione di Assistenza</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPallenberg196072_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPallenberg196072-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> At the request of Pius XII, Montini was also involved in the re-establishment of Church Asylum, providing protection to hundreds of Allied soldiers, who had escaped from Axis prison camps, Jews, anti-Fascists, Socialists, Communists, and after the liberation of Rome, German soldiers, partisans, displaced persons and others.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPallenberg196072–73_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPallenberg196072–73-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> As pope in 1971, Montini turned the <a href="/wiki/Pontificia_Commissione_di_Assistenza" title="Pontificia Commissione di Assistenza">Pontificia Commissione di Assistenza</a> into <i>Caritas Italiana</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Archbishop_of_Milan">Archbishop of Milan</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Archbishop of Milan">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cardenal_Montini.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Cardenal_Montini.jpg/195px-Cardenal_Montini.jpg" decoding="async" width="195" height="275" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Cardenal_Montini.jpg/293px-Cardenal_Montini.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Cardenal_Montini.jpg/390px-Cardenal_Montini.jpg 2x" data-file-width="496" data-file-height="699" /></a><figcaption>Montini as the Archbishop of Milan c. 1956</figcaption></figure>
<p>After the death of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Saint_Benedict" class="mw-redirect" title="Order of Saint Benedict">Benedictine</a> <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">Cardinal</a> <a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Ildefonso_Schuster" title="Alfredo Ildefonso Schuster">Alfredo Ildefonso Schuster</a>, in 1954, Montini was appointed to succeed him as <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Milan">Archbishop of Milan</a>, which made him the Secretary of the <a href="/wiki/Italian_Episcopal_Conference" class="mw-redirect" title="Italian Episcopal Conference">Italian Bishops Conference</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_420_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_420-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Pope Pius XII presented the new Archbishop Giovanni Battista Montini "as his personal gift to Milan". He was consecrated bishop in <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a> by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Eug%C3%A8ne-Gabriel-Gervais-Laurent_Tisserant" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugène-Gabriel-Gervais-Laurent Tisserant">Eugène Tisserant</a>, the Dean of the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a>, since Pius XII was forced to stay in bed due to his severe illness.
</p><p>Pius XII delivered an address about Montini's appointment from his sick-bed over radio to those assembled in St. Peter's Basilica on 12 December 1954.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini1964169_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini1964169-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Both Montini and the pope had tears in their eyes when Montini parted for his diocese, with its 1,000 churches, 2,500 priests and 3,500,000 souls.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993260–62_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993260–62-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> On 5 January 1955, Montini formally took possession of his Cathedral of Milan. After a period of settling in, Montini liked his new tasks as archbishop, connecting to all groups of faithful in Milan. He enjoyed meetings with intellectuals, artists and writers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988420_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988420-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Montini.27s_philosophy"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Montini's_philosophy">Montini's philosophy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Montini&#039;s philosophy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cardinal_Montini_portrait_%E2%80%93_1959.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Cardinal_Montini_portrait_%E2%80%93_1959.png/220px-Cardinal_Montini_portrait_%E2%80%93_1959.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="273" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Cardinal_Montini_portrait_%E2%80%93_1959.png/330px-Cardinal_Montini_portrait_%E2%80%93_1959.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Cardinal_Montini_portrait_%E2%80%93_1959.png/440px-Cardinal_Montini_portrait_%E2%80%93_1959.png 2x" data-file-width="805" data-file-height="1000" /></a><figcaption>Portrait of Cardinal Giovanni Battista Montini in 1959; photo by <a href="/wiki/Tullio_Farabola" title="Tullio Farabola">Tullio Farabola</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>In his first months Montini showed his interest in working conditions and labour issues by personally contacting unions, associations and giving related speeches. Believing that churches are the only non-utilitarian buildings in modern society and a most necessary place of spiritual rest, he initiated the building of over 100 new churches for service and contemplation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993266_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993266-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His public speeches were noticed not only in <a href="/wiki/Milan" title="Milan">Milan</a> but also in Rome and elsewhere. Some considered him a liberal, when he asked lay people to love not only Catholics but also schismatics, Protestants, Anglicans, the indifferent, Muslims, pagans, atheists.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993273_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993273-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> He gave a friendly welcome to a group of Anglican clergy visiting Milan in 1957 and subsequently exchanged letters with the <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Canterbury" title="Archbishop of Canterbury">Archbishop of Canterbury</a>, <a href="/wiki/Geoffrey_Fisher" title="Geoffrey Fisher">Geoffrey Fisher</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993714–15_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993714–15-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pope Pius XII revealed at the 1952 secret consistory that both Montini and Tardini had declined appointments to the cardinalate<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
and in fact Montini was never to be made a cardinal by Pius XII, who held no consistory and created no cardinals from the time he appointed Montini to Milan and his own death four years later. After Angelo Roncalli became Pope John XXIII, he made Montini a cardinal in December 1958.
</p><p>Montini and <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Roncalli" class="mw-redirect" title="Angelo Roncalli">Angelo Roncalli</a> were considered to be friends, but when Roncalli, as Pope John XXIII, announced a new <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Ecumenical_Councils" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic Ecumenical Councils">Ecumenical Council</a>, Cardinal Montini reacted with disbelief and said to <a href="/wiki/Giulio_Bevilacqua" title="Giulio Bevilacqua">Giulio Bevilacqua</a>: "This old boy does not know what a hornets nest he is stirring up."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993284_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993284-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> He was appointed to the Central Preparatory Commission in 1961. During the council, Pope John XXIII asked him to live in the Vatican. He was a member of the Commission for Extraordinary Affairs but did not engage himself much in the floor debates on various issues. His main advisor was <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Colombo" title="Giovanni Colombo">Giovanni Colombo</a>, whom he later appointed to be his successor in Milan<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993296_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993296-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> The commission was greatly overshadowed by the insistence of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">John XXIII</a> that the Council complete all its work in one single session before Christmas 1962, to coincide with the 400th anniversary of the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Trent" title="Council of Trent">Council of Trent</a>, an insistence which may have also been influenced by the Pope's having recently been told that he had cancer.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993301_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993301-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pastoral_progressivism">Pastoral progressivism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Pastoral progressivism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>During his period in Milan, Montini was widely seen as a progressive member of the Catholic hierarchy. He reformed pastoral care, adopting new approaches. He used his authority to ensure that the liturgical reforms of Pius XII were carried out at the local level and employed innovative methods to reach the people of Milan. For example, huge posters announced throughout the city that 1,000 voices would speak to them from 10 to 24 November 1957. More than 500 priests and many bishops, cardinals and lay people delivered 7,000 sermons in the period not only in churches but in factories, meeting halls, houses, courtyards, schools, offices, military barracks, hospitals, hotels and other places, wherever people congregated.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993275_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993275-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> His goal was the re-introduction of faith to a city without much religion. "If only we can say Our Father and know what this means, then we would understand the Christian faith."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993276_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993276-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII">Pius XII</a> asked Archbishop Montini to Rome October 1957, where he gave the main presentation to the Second World Congress of Lay Apostolate. Previously as Pro-Secretary of State, he had worked hard to form a unified worldwide organisation of lay people of 58 nations, representing 42 national organisations. He had presented them to Pius XII in Rome in 1951. The second meeting in 1957 gave Montini an opportunity to express the lay apostolate in modern terms: "Apostolate means love. We will love all, but especially those, who need help... We will love our time, our technology, our art, our sports, our world."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196463_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196463-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cardinal">Cardinal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Cardinal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_(Milano,_1962)_-_BEIC_6356260_(retouched).tif" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_%28Milano%2C_1962%29_-_BEIC_6356260_%28retouched%29.tif/lossy-page1-220px-Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_%28Milano%2C_1962%29_-_BEIC_6356260_%28retouched%29.tif.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="213" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_%28Milano%2C_1962%29_-_BEIC_6356260_%28retouched%29.tif/lossy-page1-330px-Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_%28Milano%2C_1962%29_-_BEIC_6356260_%28retouched%29.tif.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_%28Milano%2C_1962%29_-_BEIC_6356260_%28retouched%29.tif/lossy-page1-440px-Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_%28Milano%2C_1962%29_-_BEIC_6356260_%28retouched%29.tif.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1034" data-file-height="1002" /></a><figcaption>Cardinal Montini at the opening of the new building of the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Riunione_Adriatica_di_Sicurt%C3%A0&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Riunione Adriatica di Sicurtà (page does not exist)">RAS</a>, Milan, 1962; photo by <a href="/wiki/Paolo_Monti" title="Paolo Monti">Paolo Monti</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>On 20 June 1958, <a href="/wiki/Saul_Alinsky" title="Saul Alinsky">Saul Alinsky</a> recalled meeting with Montini: "I had three wonderful meetings with Montini and I am sure that you have heard from him since". Alinsky also wrote as follows to George Shuster,<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> two days before the papal conclave that elected John XXIII: "No, I don't know who the next Pope will be, but if it's to be Montini, the drinks will be on me for years to come."<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although some cardinals seem to have viewed him as <a href="/wiki/Papabile" title="Papabile">papabile</a>, a likely candidate to become pope, and although he may consequently have received some votes in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_1958" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, 1958">1958 conclave</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Montini was not yet a cardinal, which made him an unlikely choice.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Angelo Roncalli</a> was elected pope on 28 October 1958 and took the name John XXIII. On 17 November 1958, <a href="/wiki/L%27Osservatore_Romano" title="L&#39;Osservatore Romano">L'Osservatore Romano</a> announced a consistory for the creation of new cardinals. Montini's name led the list.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> When the pope raised Montini to the cardinalate on 15 December 1958, he became <a href="/wiki/Cardinal-Priest" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal-Priest">Cardinal-Priest</a> of <a href="/wiki/San_Martino_ai_Monti" title="San Martino ai Monti">Ss. Silvestro e Martino ai Monti</a>. The pope appointed him simultaneously to several Vatican congregations which resulted in many visits by Montini to Rome in the coming years.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196492_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196492-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">Cardinal</a>, Montini journeyed to Africa (1962), where he visited <a href="/wiki/Ghana" title="Ghana">Ghana</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sudan" title="Sudan">Sudan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kenya" title="Kenya">Kenya</a>, <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_the_Congo_(L%C3%A9opoldville)" title="Republic of the Congo (Léopoldville)">Congo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rhodesia" title="Rhodesia">Rhodesia</a>, South Africa, and Nigeria. After this journey, John XXIII called Montini to a private audience for a debriefing on his trip which lasted for several hours. In fifteen other trips he visited <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a> (1960) and the USA (1960), including New York City, Washington DC, Chicago, the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Notre_Dame" title="University of Notre Dame">University of Notre Dame</a> in Indiana, Boston, <a href="/wiki/Philadelphia" title="Philadelphia">Philadelphia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Baltimore" title="Baltimore">Baltimore</a>. While a cardinal, he usually vacationed in <a href="/wiki/Engelberg_Abbey" title="Engelberg Abbey">Engelberg Abbey</a>, a secluded Benedictine monastery in Switzerland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELazzarini196490–92_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELazzarini196490–92-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Papacy">Papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Paul VI</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Coat_of_Arms_of_Pope_Paul_VI_(G._Montini).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Coat_of_Arms_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%28G._Montini%29.svg/60px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%28G._Montini%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="87" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Coat_of_Arms_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%28G._Montini%29.svg/90px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%28G._Montini%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Coat_of_Arms_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%28G._Montini%29.svg/120px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%28G._Montini%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="724" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">Saint</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Papal_conclave">Papal conclave</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Papal conclave">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1963_papal_conclave" title="1963 papal conclave">1963 papal conclave</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Election_of_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Election_of_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg/220px-Election_of_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="349" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Election_of_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg/330px-Election_of_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Election_of_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg/440px-Election_of_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1210" data-file-height="1920" /></a><figcaption>Pope Paul VI appears on the central loggia after his election on 21 June 1963</figcaption></figure>
<p>Montini was generally seen as the <a href="/wiki/Papabile" title="Papabile">most likely successor</a> to Pope John XXIII because of his closeness to both Popes <a href="/wiki/Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII">Pius XII</a> and John XXIII, his pastoral and administrative background, and his insight and determination.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997275_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997275-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> John XXIII was not exactly a newcomer to the Vatican, since he had been an official of the Holy See in Rome and until his appointment to Venice was a papal diplomat, but returning to Rome at the age of 66 he may have felt outflanked by the professional <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a> at times; Montini knew its most inner workings well due to the fact that he had worked there for a generation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997275_63-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997275-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Unlike the <a href="/wiki/Papabile" title="Papabile">papabile</a> cardinals <a href="/wiki/Giacomo_Lercaro" title="Giacomo Lercaro">Giacomo Lercaro</a> of <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a> and <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Siri" title="Giuseppe Siri">Giuseppe Siri</a> of <a href="/wiki/Genoa" title="Genoa">Genoa</a>, Montini was not identified with either the left or right, nor was he seen as a radical reformer. He was viewed as most likely to continue the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997275_63-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997275-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> which already, without any tangible results, had lasted longer than John XXIII expected. John had a vision but "did not have a clear agenda. His rhetoric seems to have had a note of over-optimism, a confidence in progress, which was characteristic of the 1960s."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997272_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997272-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> When John XXIII died of <a href="/wiki/Stomach_cancer" title="Stomach cancer">stomach cancer</a> on 3 June 1963, this triggered a conclave to elect a new pope.
</p><p>Montini was elected pope on the sixth ballot of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_1963" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, 1963">papal conclave</a> on 21 June and he took the name of "Paul VI". When the <a href="/wiki/Dean_of_the_College_of_Cardinals" title="Dean of the College of Cardinals">Dean of the College of Cardinals</a> <a href="/wiki/Eug%C3%A8ne_Tisserant" title="Eugène Tisserant">Eugène Tisserant</a> asked if he accepted the election, Montini said <i>"Accepto, in nomine Domini"</i> ("I accept, in the name of the Lord"). At one point during the conclave on 20 June, it was said, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Gustavo_Testa" title="Gustavo Testa">Gustavo Testa</a> lost his temper and demanded that opponents of Montini halt their efforts to thwart his election.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> It was following Testa's outburst that Montini, fearful of causing a division, started to rise in order to dissuade the cardinals from voting for him. However, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Urbani" title="Giovanni Urbani">Giovanni Urbani</a> dragged Montini back to his seat, muttering, "Eminence, shut up!"<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Montini took the name "Paul" in honour of <a href="/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle" title="Paul the Apostle">Paul the Apostle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The white smoke first rose from the chimney of the Sistine Chapel at 11:22 am. Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Ottaviani" title="Alfredo Ottaviani">Alfredo Ottaviani</a>, in his role as <a href="/wiki/Protodeacon" title="Protodeacon">Protodeacon</a>, announced to the public the successful election of Montini. When the new pope appeared on the central loggia, he gave the shorter <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_blessing" title="Episcopal blessing">episcopal blessing</a> as his first <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Blessing" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Blessing">Apostolic Blessing</a> rather than the longer, traditional <i><a href="/wiki/Urbi_et_Orbi" title="Urbi et Orbi">Urbi et Orbi</a></i>.
</p><p>Of the papacy, Paul VI wrote in his journal: "The position is unique. It brings great solitude. 'I was solitary before, but now my solitude becomes complete and awesome.'"<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993339_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993339-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Less than two years later, on 2 May 1965, Paul addressed a letter to the dean of the College of Cardinals anticipating that his health might make it impossible to function as pope. He wrote that "In case of infirmity, which is believed to be incurable or is of long duration and which impedes us from sufficiently exercising the functions of our apostolic ministry; or in the case of another serious and prolonged impediment", he would renounce his office "both as bishop of Rome as well as head of the same holy Catholic Church".<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Reforms_of_papal_ceremony">Reforms of papal ceremony</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Reforms of papal ceremony">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Paul_VI_portrait.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Pope_Paul_VI_portrait.jpg/220px-Pope_Paul_VI_portrait.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="285" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Pope_Paul_VI_portrait.jpg/330px-Pope_Paul_VI_portrait.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Pope_Paul_VI_portrait.jpg/440px-Pope_Paul_VI_portrait.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1120" data-file-height="1452" /></a><figcaption>One of the first official photographs of Paul VI taken in 1963<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>Paul VI did away with much of the regal splendor of the papacy. <a href="/wiki/Coronation_of_Pope_Paul_VI" title="Coronation of Pope Paul VI">His coronation</a> on 30 June 1963 was the last such ceremony;<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> his successor <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I" title="Pope John Paul I">Pope John Paul I</a> substituted an <a href="/wiki/Papal_inauguration" title="Papal inauguration">inauguration</a> for the <a href="/wiki/Papal_coronation" title="Papal coronation">papal coronation</a> (which Paul had substantially modified, but which he left mandatory in his 1975 <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_constitution" title="Apostolic constitution">apostolic constitution</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Romano_Pontifici_Eligendo" class="mw-redirect" title="Romano Pontifici Eligendo">Romano Pontifici Eligendo</a></i>). At his coronation Paul wore a <a href="/wiki/Papal_tiara" title="Papal tiara">tiara</a> that was a gift from the Archdiocese of Milan. At the end of the second session of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> in 1963, Paul VI descended the steps of the papal throne in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a> and ascended to the altar, on which he laid the tiara as a sign of the renunciation of human glory and power in keeping with the renewed spirit of the council. It was announced that the tiara would be sold and the money obtained would be given to charity.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> The purchasers arranged for it to be displayed as a gift to American Catholics in the crypt of the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception" title="Basilica of the National Shrine of the Immaculate Conception">Basilica of the National Shrine of the Immaculate Conception</a> in Washington, D.C.
</p><p>In 1968, with the <a href="/wiki/Motu_proprio" title="Motu proprio">motu proprio</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Pontificalis_Domus" title="Pontificalis Domus">Pontificalis Domus</a></i>, he discontinued most of the ceremonial functions of the old Roman nobility at the court (reorganized as the <a href="/wiki/Papal_household" title="Papal household">household</a>), save for the <a href="/wiki/Prince_Assistants_to_the_Papal_Throne" class="mw-redirect" title="Prince Assistants to the Papal Throne">Prince Assistants to the Papal Throne</a>. He also abolished the <a href="/wiki/Palatine_Guard" title="Palatine Guard">Palatine Guard</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Noble_Guard_(Vatican)" class="mw-redirect" title="Noble Guard (Vatican)">Noble Guard</a>, leaving the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Swiss_Guard" class="mw-redirect" title="Pontifical Swiss Guard">Pontifical Swiss Guard</a> as the sole military order of the Vatican.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Completion_of_the_Vatican_Council">Completion of the Vatican Council</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Completion of the Vatican Council">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a></div>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:CardinalBea.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/CardinalBea.jpg/150px-CardinalBea.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/CardinalBea.jpg/225px-CardinalBea.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/CardinalBea.jpg/300px-CardinalBea.jpg 2x" data-file-width="591" data-file-height="887" /></a><figcaption>Pope Paul VI fully supported Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Augustin_Bea" title="Augustin Bea">Augustin Bea</a>, credited with ecumenical breakthroughs during the Second Vatican Council.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Paul VI decided to continue <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Vatican II</a> (canon law dictates that a council is suspended at the death of a pope), and brought it to completion in 1965. Faced with conflicting interpretations and controversies, he directed the implementation of its reform goals.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Ecumenical_orientation">Ecumenical orientation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Ecumenical orientation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI_and_Ecumenism" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope Paul VI and Ecumenism">Pope Paul VI and Ecumenism</a></div>
<p>During Vatican II, the Council Fathers avoided statements which might anger Christians of other faiths.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific page or range of pages in which the material appears. (January 2014)">page&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Augustin_Bea" title="Augustin Bea">Augustin Bea</a>, the President of the Christian Unity Secretariat, always had the full support of Paul VI in his attempts to ensure that the Council language was friendly and open to the sensitivities of Protestant and Orthodox Churches, whom he had invited to all sessions at the request of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Pope John XXIII</a>. Bea also was strongly involved in the passage of <i><a href="/wiki/Nostra_aetate" title="Nostra aetate">Nostra aetate</a></i>, which regulates the church's relations with the Jewish faith and members of other religions.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Dialogue_with_the_world">Dialogue with the world</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Dialogue with the world">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>After his election as Bishop of Rome, Paul VI first met with the priests in his new diocese. He told them that in Milan he started a dialogue with the modern world and asked them to seek contact with all people from all walks of life. Six days after his election he announced that he would continue Vatican II and convened the opening to take place on 29 September 1963.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_420_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_420-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> In a radio address to the world, Paul VI recalled the uniqueness of his predecessors, the strength of <a href="/wiki/Pius_XI" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XI">Pius XI</a>, the wisdom and intelligence of <a href="/wiki/Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII">Pius XII</a> and the love of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">John XXIII</a>. As "his pontifical goals" he mentioned the continuation and completion of Vatican II, the reform of the <a href="/wiki/Canon_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon Law">Canon Law</a> and improved social peace and justice in the world. The Unity of Christianity would be central to his activities.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_420_41-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_420-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Council_priorities">Council priorities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Council priorities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The pope re-opened the Ecumenical Council on 29 September 1963 giving it four key priorities:
</p>
<ul><li>A better understanding of the Catholic Church</li>
<li>Church reforms</li>
<li>Advancing the unity of Christianity</li>
<li>Dialogue with the world<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_420_41-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_420-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MontiniKennedy.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/MontiniKennedy.jpg/255px-MontiniKennedy.jpg" decoding="async" width="255" height="153" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/MontiniKennedy.jpg/383px-MontiniKennedy.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/MontiniKennedy.jpg/510px-MontiniKennedy.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1224" data-file-height="732" /></a><figcaption>Pope Paul VI meets with the first Catholic U.S. president, <a href="/wiki/John_F._Kennedy" title="John F. Kennedy">John F. Kennedy</a>, 2 July 1963</figcaption></figure>
<p>He reminded the council fathers that only a few years earlier Pope Pius XII had issued the encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Mystici_corporis" class="mw-redirect" title="Mystici corporis">Mystici corporis</a></i> about the mystical body of Christ. He asked them not to repeat or create new dogmatic definitions but to explain in simple words how the church sees itself. He thanked the representatives of other Christian communities for their attendance and asked for their forgiveness if the Catholic Church is guilty for the separation. He also reminded the Council Fathers that many bishops from the east could not attend because the governments in the East did not permit their journeys.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988421–22_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988421–22-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Konzilseroeffnung_1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Konzilseroeffnung_1.jpg/255px-Konzilseroeffnung_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="255" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Konzilseroeffnung_1.jpg/383px-Konzilseroeffnung_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Konzilseroeffnung_1.jpg/510px-Konzilseroeffnung_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2500" data-file-height="1666" /></a><figcaption>The opening of the second session of Vatican II</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Third_and_fourth_sessions">Third and fourth sessions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Third and fourth sessions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Paul VI opened the third period on 14 September 1964, telling the Council Fathers that he viewed the text about the church as the most important document to come out from the council. As the Council discussed the role of bishops in the papacy, Paul VI issued an <a href="/wiki/Nota_Praevia" class="mw-redirect" title="Nota Praevia">explanatory note</a> confirming the primacy of the papacy, a step which was viewed by some as meddling in the affairs of the Council.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988423_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988423-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> American bishops pushed for a speedy resolution on religious freedom, but Paul VI insisted this to be approved together with related texts such as ecumenism.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_424_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_424-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> The Pope concluded the session on 21 November 1964, with the formal pronouncement of Mary as <a href="/wiki/Mother_of_the_Church" title="Mother of the Church">Mother of the Church</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_424_77-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_424-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Between the third and fourth sessions the pope announced reforms in the areas of <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a>, revision of <a href="/wiki/Canon_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon Law">Canon Law</a>, regulations for <a href="/wiki/Interfaith_marriage" title="Interfaith marriage">mixed marriages</a> involving several faiths, and <a href="/wiki/Birth_control" title="Birth control">birth control</a> issues. He opened the final session of the council, concelebrating with bishops from countries where the church was persecuted. Several texts proposed for his approval had to be changed. But all texts were finally agreed upon. The council was concluded on 8 December 1965, the Feast of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_424_77-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_424-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the final session of the council, Paul VI announced that he would open the canonisation processes of his immediate predecessors: Pope Pius XII and Pope John XXIII.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Universal_call_to_holiness">Universal call to holiness</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Universal call to holiness">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>According to Pope Paul VI, "the most characteristic and ultimate purpose of the teachings of the Council" is the <a href="/wiki/Universal_call_to_holiness" title="Universal call to holiness">universal call to holiness</a>:<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> "all the faithful of Christ of whatever rank or status, are called to the fullness of the Christian life and to the perfection of charity; by this holiness as such a more human manner of living is promoted in this earthly society." This teaching is found in <i><a href="/wiki/Lumen_Gentium" class="mw-redirect" title="Lumen Gentium">Lumen Gentium</a></i>, the Dogmatic Constitution on the Church, promulgated by Paul VI on 21 November 1964.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Church_reforms">Church reforms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Church reforms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:AmbroseOfMilan.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/AmbroseOfMilan.jpg/140px-AmbroseOfMilan.jpg" decoding="async" width="140" height="269" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/AmbroseOfMilan.jpg/210px-AmbroseOfMilan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/AmbroseOfMilan.jpg/280px-AmbroseOfMilan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="312" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>Following his predecessor <a href="/wiki/Ambrose_of_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="Ambrose of Milan">Ambrose of Milan</a>, Pope Paul VI named <a href="/wiki/Veneration_of_Mary_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Veneration of Mary in the Catholic Church">Mary</a> the "<a href="/wiki/Mother_of_the_Church" title="Mother of the Church">Mother of the Church</a>" during <a href="/wiki/Vatican_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican II">Vatican II</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Synod_of_Bishops">Synod of Bishops</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Synod of Bishops">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>On 14 September 1965, he established the <a href="/wiki/Synod_of_Bishops_(Catholic)" class="mw-redirect" title="Synod of Bishops (Catholic)">Synod of Bishops</a> as a permanent institution of the Catholic Church and an advisory body to the papacy. Several meetings were held on specific issues during his pontificate, such as the Synod of Bishops on evangelisation in the modern world, which started 9 September 1974.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_425_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_425-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Curia_reform">Curia reform</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Curia reform">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI%27s_reform_of_the_Roman_Curia" title="Pope Paul VI&#39;s reform of the Roman Curia">Pope Paul VI's reform of the Roman Curia</a></div>
<p>Pope Paul VI knew the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a> well, having worked there for a generation from 1922 to 1954. He implemented his reforms in stages. On 1 March 1968, he issued a regulation, a process that had been initiated by Pius XII and continued by John XXIII. On 28 March, with <i>Pontificalis Domus</i>, and in several additional Apostolic Constitutions in the following years, he revamped the entire Curia, which included reduction of bureaucracy, streamlining of existing congregations and a broader representation of non-Italians in the curial positions.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Age_limits_and_restrictions">Age limits and restrictions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Age limits and restrictions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>On 6 August 1966, Paul VI asked all bishops to submit their resignations to the pontiff by their 75th birthday. They were not required to do so but "earnestly requested of their own free will to tender their resignation from office".<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> He extended this requirement to all cardinals in <i><a href="/wiki/Ingravescentem_aetatem" title="Ingravescentem aetatem">Ingravescentem aetatem</a></i> on 21 November 1970, with the further provision that cardinals would relinquish their offices in the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a> upon reaching their 80th birthday.<sup id="cite_ref-IA_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IA-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> These retirement rules enabled the Pope to fill several positions with younger prelates and reduce the Italian domination of the Roman Curia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988425_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988425-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> His 1970 measures also revolutionised papal elections by restricting the right to vote in <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave" title="Papal conclave">papal conclaves</a> to cardinals who had not yet reached their 80th birthday, a class known since then as "cardinal electors". This reduced the power of the Italians and the Curia in the next conclave. Some senior cardinals objected to losing their voting privilege, without effect.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
Paul VI's measures also limited the number of cardinal-electors to a maximum of 120,<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> a rule disregarded on several occasions by his successors.
</p><p>Some prelates questioned whether he should not apply these retirement rules to himself.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> When Pope Paul was asked towards the end of his papacy whether he would retire at age 80, he replied "Kings can abdicate, Popes cannot."<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Liturgy">Liturgy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Liturgy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mass_of_Paul_VI" title="Mass of Paul VI">Mass of Paul VI</a></div>
<p>Reform of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_liturgy" title="Catholic liturgy">liturgy</a>, an aim of the 20th-century <a href="/wiki/Liturgical_movement" class="mw-redirect" title="Liturgical movement">liturgical movement</a>, mainly in France and Germany, was officially recognised as legitimate by Pius XII in his encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Mediator_Dei" title="Mediator Dei">Mediator Dei</a></i>. During his pontificate, he eased regulations on the obligatory use of Latin in Catholic liturgies, permitting some use of vernacular languages during baptisms, funerals and other events. In 1951 and 1955, he revised the Easter liturgies, most notably that of the <a href="/wiki/Easter_Triduum" class="mw-redirect" title="Easter Triduum">Easter Triduum</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdam198547–48_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdam198547–48-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> (1962–1965) gave some directives in its document <i><a href="/wiki/Sacrosanctum_Concilium" title="Sacrosanctum Concilium">Sacrosanctum Concilium</a></i> for a general revision of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Missal" title="Roman Missal">Roman Missal</a>. Within four years of the close of the council, Paul VI promulgated in 1969 the first postconciliar edition, which included three new <a href="/wiki/Eucharistic_Prayer" class="mw-redirect" title="Eucharistic Prayer">Eucharistic Prayers</a> in addition to the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Canon" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Canon">Roman Canon</a>, until then the only <a href="/wiki/Anaphora_(liturgy)" title="Anaphora (liturgy)">anaphora</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Rite" title="Roman Rite">Roman Rite</a>. Use of <a href="/wiki/Vernacular" title="Vernacular">vernacular</a> languages was expanded by decision of <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_conference" title="Episcopal conference">episcopal conferences</a>, not by papal command. In addition to his revision of the Roman Missal, Pope Paul VI issued instructions in 1964, 1967, 1968, 1969 and 1970, reforming other elements of the liturgy of the Roman Church.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdam198549_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdam198549-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>These reforms were not universally welcomed. Questions were raised about the need to replace the <a href="/wiki/1962_Roman_Missal" class="mw-redirect" title="1962 Roman Missal">1962 Roman Missal</a>, which, though decreed on 23 June 1962<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> became available only in 1963, a few months before the Second Vatican Council's <i>Sacrosanctum Concilium</i> decree ordered that it be altered;<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> but attachment to it led to open ruptures, of which the most widely known is that of <a href="/wiki/Marcel_Lefebvre" title="Marcel Lefebvre">Marcel Lefebvre</a>. <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> granted bishops the right to authorise use of the 1962 Missal (<i><a href="/wiki/Quattuor_abhinc_annos" title="Quattuor abhinc annos">Quattuor abhinc annos</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Ecclesia_Dei" title="Ecclesia Dei">Ecclesia Dei</a></i>) and in 2007 <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a>, while stating that the Mass of Paul VI and John Paul II "obviously is and continues to be the normal Form – the <i>Forma ordinaria</i> – of the Eucharistic Liturgy",<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> gave general permission to priests of the <a href="/wiki/Latin_Church" title="Latin Church">Latin Church</a> to use either the 1962 Missal or the post-<a href="/wiki/Vatican_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican II">Vatican II</a> Missal both privately and, under certain conditions, publicly.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> In 2021, Pope Francis removed many of faculties granted by Pope Benedict XVI with the publishing of his motu proprio, <i><a href="/wiki/Traditionis_custodes" title="Traditionis custodes">Traditionis Custodes</a></i>, thus limiting the use of 1962 Roman Missal.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relations_and_dialogues">Relations and dialogues</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Relations and dialogues">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
