<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:General_audience_of_Pope_Paul_VI_(1973).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/General_audience_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%281973%29.jpg/200px-General_audience_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%281973%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="233" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/General_audience_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%281973%29.jpg/300px-General_audience_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%281973%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/General_audience_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%281973%29.jpg/400px-General_audience_of_Pope_Paul_VI_%281973%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="786" data-file-height="915" /></a><figcaption>Pope Paul VI during an October 1973 audience</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paulus_VI_visit_to_Israel,_Mount_Tabor_1964_(997009326801305171).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Pope Paul VI at Mount Tabor, during his 1964 visit to Israel" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Paulus_VI_visit_to_Israel%2C_Mount_Tabor_1964_%28997009326801305171%29.jpg/220px-Paulus_VI_visit_to_Israel%2C_Mount_Tabor_1964_%28997009326801305171%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="224" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Paulus_VI_visit_to_Israel%2C_Mount_Tabor_1964_%28997009326801305171%29.jpg/330px-Paulus_VI_visit_to_Israel%2C_Mount_Tabor_1964_%28997009326801305171%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Paulus_VI_visit_to_Israel%2C_Mount_Tabor_1964_%28997009326801305171%29.jpg/440px-Paulus_VI_visit_to_Israel%2C_Mount_Tabor_1964_%28997009326801305171%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1596" data-file-height="1625" /></a><figcaption>Pope Paul VI at <a href="/wiki/Mount_Tabor" title="Mount Tabor">Mount Tabor</a>, during his 1964 visit to Israel</figcaption></figure>
<p>To Paul VI, a dialogue with all of humanity was essential not as an aim but as a means to find the truth. Dialogue according to Paul, is based on full equality of all participants. This equality is rooted in the common search for the truth.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967172_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967172-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> He said: "Those who have the truth, are in a position as not having it, because they are forced to search for it every day in a deeper and more perfect way. Those who do not have it, but search for it with their whole heart, have already found it."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967172_96-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967172-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Dialogues">Dialogues</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Dialogues">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Jafar_Shahidi_and_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Jafar_Shahidi_and_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg/220px-Jafar_Shahidi_and_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="159" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Jafar_Shahidi_and_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg/330px-Jafar_Shahidi_and_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Jafar_Shahidi_and_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg/440px-Jafar_Shahidi_and_Pope_Paul_VI.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="723" /></a><figcaption>Pope Paul VI meets <a href="/wiki/Jafar_Shahidi" title="Jafar Shahidi">Jafar Shahidi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Seyyed_Hossein_Nasr" title="Seyyed Hossein Nasr">Hossein Nasr</a>, Iranian Shia scholars.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1964, Paul VI created a Secretariat for non-Christians, later renamed the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Council_for_Interreligious_Dialogue" class="mw-redirect" title="Pontifical Council for Interreligious Dialogue">Pontifical Council for Interreligious Dialogue</a> and a year later a new Secretariat (later Pontifical Council) for Dialogue with Non-Believers. This latter was in 1993 incorporated by Pope John Paul II in the Pontifical Council for Culture, which he had established in 1982. In 1971, Paul VI created a papal office for economic development and catastrophic assistance. To foster common bonds with all persons of good will, he decreed an annual peace day to be celebrated on January first of every year. Trying to improve the condition of Christians behind the Iron Curtain, Paul VI engaged in dialogue with Communist authorities at several levels, receiving Foreign Minister <a href="/wiki/Andrei_Gromyko" title="Andrei Gromyko">Andrei Gromyko</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_heads_of_state_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="List of heads of state of the Soviet Union">Chairman</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Presidium_of_the_Supreme_Soviet" title="Presidium of the Supreme Soviet">Presidium of the Supreme Soviet</a> <a href="/wiki/Nikolai_Podgorny" title="Nikolai Podgorny">Nikolai Podgorny</a> in 1966 and 1967 in the Vatican. The situation of the church in Hungary, Poland and Romania, improved during his pontificate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988427_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1988427-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_travels">Foreign travels</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Foreign travels">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Foreign_trips_of_Paul_VI.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Foreign_trips_of_Paul_VI.svg/195px-Foreign_trips_of_Paul_VI.svg.png" decoding="async" width="195" height="99" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Foreign_trips_of_Paul_VI.svg/293px-Foreign_trips_of_Paul_VI.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Foreign_trips_of_Paul_VI.svg/390px-Foreign_trips_of_Paul_VI.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="260" /></a><figcaption>The countries visited by Pope Paul VI</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Relief_of_Paulus_VI_in_Nazareth.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Relief_of_Paulus_VI_in_Nazareth.JPG/195px-Relief_of_Paulus_VI_in_Nazareth.JPG" decoding="async" width="195" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Relief_of_Paulus_VI_in_Nazareth.JPG/293px-Relief_of_Paulus_VI_in_Nazareth.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Relief_of_Paulus_VI_in_Nazareth.JPG/390px-Relief_of_Paulus_VI_in_Nazareth.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4608" data-file-height="3456" /></a><figcaption>Relief commemorating Pope Paul VI's visit to Nazareth, 5 January 1964</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Paul_VI%27s_Diamond_Ring_and_Cross.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Pope_Paul_VI%27s_Diamond_Ring_and_Cross.jpg/220px-Pope_Paul_VI%27s_Diamond_Ring_and_Cross.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="232" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Pope_Paul_VI%27s_Diamond_Ring_and_Cross.jpg/330px-Pope_Paul_VI%27s_Diamond_Ring_and_Cross.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Pope_Paul_VI%27s_Diamond_Ring_and_Cross.jpg/440px-Pope_Paul_VI%27s_Diamond_Ring_and_Cross.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1414" data-file-height="1488" /></a><figcaption>Pope Paul VI's diamond ring and cross donated to the United Nations</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_pastoral_visits_of_Pope_Paul_VI_outside_Italy" class="mw-redirect" title="List of pastoral visits of Pope Paul VI outside Italy">List of pastoral visits of Pope Paul VI outside Italy</a></div>
<p>Pope Paul VI became the first pope to visit six continents. He was also the first pontiff to travel on an airplane, visit the Holy Land on pilgrimage, and travel outside of Italy in a century. He travelled more widely than any of his predecessors, earning the nickname "the Pilgrim Pope". He visited the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Land" title="Holy Land">Holy Land</a> in 1964 and participated in Eucharistic Congresses in <a href="/wiki/Bombay" class="mw-redirect" title="Bombay">Bombay</a>, India and <a href="/wiki/Bogot%C3%A1" title="Bogotá">Bogotá</a>, Colombia. In 1966, he was twice denied permission to visit <a href="/wiki/Poland" title="Poland">Poland</a> for the 1,000th anniversary of the <a href="/wiki/Christianization_of_Poland" title="Christianization of Poland">introduction of Christianity in Poland</a>. In 1967, he visited the shrine of <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_F%C3%A1tima" title="Our Lady of Fátima">Our Lady of Fátima</a> in <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a> on the fiftieth anniversary of the apparitions there. He undertook a pastoral visit to Uganda in 1969,<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> the first by a reigning pope to Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> On 27 November 1970 he was the target of an assassination attempt at <a href="/wiki/Ninoy_Aquino_International_Airport" title="Ninoy Aquino International Airport">Manila International Airport</a> in the Philippines. He was only lightly stabbed by <a href="/wiki/Benjam%C3%ADn_Mendoza_y_Amor_Flores" title="Benjamín Mendoza y Amor Flores">Benjamín Mendoza y Amor Flores</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> who was subdued by the pope's personal bodyguard and travel organiser, <a href="/wiki/Paul_Marcinkus" title="Paul Marcinkus">Paul Marcinkus</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-manila_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-manila-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The source does not mention Marcinkus. (June 2022)">failed verification</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Pope Paul VI became the first reigning pontiff to visit the Western hemisphere when he addressed the United Nations in New York City in October 1965.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> As the U.S. involvement in the <a href="/wiki/Vietnam_War" title="Vietnam War">Vietnam War</a> was escalating, Paul VI pleaded for peace before the UN:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Our very brief visit has given us a great honour; that of proclaiming to the whole world, from the Headquarters of the United Nations, Peace! We shall never forget this extraordinary hour. Nor can We bring it to a more fitting conclusion than by expressing the wish that this central seat of human relationships for the civil peace of the world may ever be conscious and worthy of this high privilege.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>No more war, never again war. Peace, it is peace that must guide the destinies of people and of all mankind."<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Attempted_assassination">Attempted assassination</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Attempted assassination">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Shortly after arriving at the airport in Manila, Philippines on 27 November 1970, the Pope, closely followed by President <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_Marcos" title="Ferdinand Marcos">Ferdinand Marcos</a> and personal aide <a href="/wiki/Pasquale_Macchi" title="Pasquale Macchi">Pasquale Macchi</a>, who was private secretary to Pope Paul VI, were encountered suddenly by a crew-cut, cassock-clad man who tried to attack the Pope with a knife. Macchi pushed the man away; police identified the would-be assassin as Benjamin Mendoza y Amor, 35, of La Paz, Bolivia. Mendoza was an artist living in the Philippines. The Pontiff continued with his trip and thanked Marcos and Macchi, who both had moved to protect him during the attack.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="New_diplomacy">New diplomacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: New diplomacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Like his predecessor <a href="/wiki/Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII">Pius XII</a>, Paul VI put much emphasis on the dialogue with all nations of the world through establishing diplomatic relations. The number of foreign embassies accredited to the Vatican doubled during his pontificate.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_430_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_430-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> This was a reflection of a new understanding between church and state, which had been formulated first by <a href="/wiki/Pius_XI" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XI">Pius XI</a> and Pius XII but decreed by Vatican II. The pastoral constitution <i><a href="/wiki/Gaudium_et_spes" title="Gaudium et spes">Gaudium et spes</a></i> stated that the Catholic Church is not bound to any form of government and willing to co-operate with all forms. The church maintained its right to select bishops on its own without any interference by the State.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1991391_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1991391-112">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pope Paul VI sent one of 73 <a href="/wiki/Apollo_11_Goodwill_Messages" class="mw-redirect" title="Apollo 11 Goodwill Messages">Apollo 11 Goodwill Messages</a> to <a href="/wiki/NASA" title="NASA">NASA</a> for the historic first lunar landing. The message still rests on the lunar surface today. It has the words of the <a href="/wiki/Psalm_8" title="Psalm 8">8th Psalm</a> and the pope wrote, "To the Glory of the name of God who gives such power to men, we ardently pray for this wonderful beginning."<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Theology">Theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Mariology">Mariology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Mariology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mariology_of_Pope_Paul_VI" class="mw-redirect" title="Mariology of Pope Paul VI">Mariology of Pope Paul VI</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paulo_VI_em_F%C3%A1tima.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Paulo_VI_em_F%C3%A1tima.jpg/220px-Paulo_VI_em_F%C3%A1tima.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="166" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Paulo_VI_em_F%C3%A1tima.jpg/330px-Paulo_VI_em_F%C3%A1tima.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Paulo_VI_em_F%C3%A1tima.jpg/440px-Paulo_VI_em_F%C3%A1tima.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1433" data-file-height="1080" /></a><figcaption>Paul VI during his visit to the <a href="/wiki/Sanctuary_of_F%C3%A1tima" title="Sanctuary of Fátima">Sanctuary of Fátima</a> in 1967</figcaption></figure>
<p>Pope Paul VI made extensive contributions to <a href="/wiki/Mariology" title="Mariology">Mariology</a> (theological teaching and devotions) during his pontificate. He attempted to present the Marian teachings of the church in view of her new ecumenical orientation. In his inaugural encyclical <i>Ecclesiam suam</i> (section below), the pope called Mary the ideal of Christian perfection. He regards "devotion to the Mother of God as of paramount importance in living the life of the Gospel."<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Encyclicals">Encyclicals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Encyclicals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Paul VI authored seven encyclicals.
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Ecclesiam_suam"><i>Ecclesiam suam</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Ecclesiam suam">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Ecclesiam_suam" class="mw-redirect" title="Ecclesiam suam">Ecclesiam suam</a></div>
<p><i>Ecclesiam suam</i> was given at St. Peter's, Rome, on the Feast of the Transfiguration, 6 August 1964, the second year of his Pontificate. It is considered an important document, identifying the Catholic Church with the Body of Christ. A later Council document <i>Lumen Gentium</i> stated that the church subsists in the Body of Christ, raising questions as to the difference between "is" and "subsists in". Paul VI appealed to "all people of good will" and discussed necessary dialogues within the church and between the churches and with atheism.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_425_79-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_425-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Mense_maio"><i>Mense maio</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Mense maio">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mense_maio" title="Mense maio">Mense maio</a></div>
<p>The encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Mense_maio" title="Mense maio">Mense maio</a></i> (from 29 April 1965) focused on the Virgin Mary, to whom traditionally the month of May is dedicated as the Mother of God. Paul VI writes that Mary is rightly to be regarded as the way by which people are led to Christ. Therefore, the person who encounters Mary cannot help but encounter Christ.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Mysterium_fidei"><i>Mysterium fidei</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Mysterium fidei">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mysterium_fidei_(encyclical)" title="Mysterium fidei (encyclical)">Mysterium fidei (encyclical)</a></div>
<p>On 3 September 1965, Paul VI issued <i>Mysterium fidei</i>, on the mystery of the faith. He opposed relativistic notions which would have given the <a href="/wiki/Eucharist" title="Eucharist">Eucharist</a> a symbolic character only. The church, according to Paul VI, has no reason to give up the deposit of faith in such a vital matter.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_425_79-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_425-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Christi_Matri"><i>Christi Matri</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Christi Matri">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Christi_Matri" title="Christi Matri">Christi Matri</a></div>
<p>On 15 September 1966, Paul VI issued <i>Christi Matri</i>, a request of the faithful to pray for peace during the month of October 1966. As reasons for this call to prayer, Paul VI alludes to the Vietnam War and lists concern about "the growing nuclear armaments race, the senseless nationalism, the racism, the obsession for revolution, the separations imposed upon citizens, the nefarious plots, the slaughter of innocent people."<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Populorum_progressio"><i>Populorum progressio</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Populorum progressio">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Populorum_progressio" title="Populorum progressio">Populorum progressio</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paulaudenece1977.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Paulaudenece1977.jpg/180px-Paulaudenece1977.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="257" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Paulaudenece1977.jpg/270px-Paulaudenece1977.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Paulaudenece1977.jpg/360px-Paulaudenece1977.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1241" data-file-height="1773" /></a><figcaption>Paul VI at an audience in October 1977</figcaption></figure>
<p><i>Populorum progressio</i>, released on 26 March 1967, dealt with the topic of "the development of peoples" and that the economy of the world should serve mankind and not just the few. It touches on a variety of traditional principles of Catholic social teaching: the right to a just wage; the right to security of employment; the right to fair and reasonable working conditions; the right to join a union and strike as a last resort; and the <a href="/wiki/Universal_destination_of_resources_and_goods" class="mw-redirect" title="Universal destination of resources and goods">universal destination of resources and goods</a>.
</p><p>In addition, <i>Populorum progressio</i> opines that real peace in the world is conditional on justice. He repeats his demands expressed in Bombay in 1964 for a large-scale World Development Organization, as a matter of international justice and peace. He rejected notions to instigate revolution and force in changing economic conditions.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Sacerdotalis_caelibatus"><i>Sacerdotalis caelibatus</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Sacerdotalis caelibatus">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sacerdotalis_caelibatus" title="Sacerdotalis caelibatus">Sacerdotalis caelibatus</a></div>
<p><i>Sacerdotalis caelibatus</i> (Latin for "Of the celibate priesthood"), promulgated on 24 June 1967, defends the Catholic Church's tradition of priestly celibacy in the West. This encyclical was written in the wake of Vatican II, when the Catholic Church was questioning and revising many long-held practices. Priestly celibacy is considered a <a href="/wiki/Discipline" title="Discipline">discipline</a> rather than <a href="/wiki/Dogma" title="Dogma">dogma</a>, and some had expected that it might be relaxed. In response to these questions, the Pope reaffirms the discipline as a long-held practice with special importance in the Catholic Church. The encyclical <i>Sacerdotalis caelibatus</i> from 24 June 1967, confirms the traditional church teaching, that celibacy is an ideal state and continues to be mandatory for Catholic priests. Celibacy symbolises the reality of the kingdom of God amid modern society. The priestly celibacy is closely linked to the sacramental priesthood.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_425_79-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_425-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> However, during his pontificate Paul VI was permissive in allowing bishops to grant laicisation of priests who wanted to leave the sacerdotal state. <a href="/wiki/John_Paul_II" class="mw-redirect" title="John Paul II">John Paul II</a> changed this policy in 1980 and the 1983 Code of <a href="/wiki/Canon_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon Law">Canon Law</a> made it explicit that only the pope can in exceptional circumstances grant laicisation.
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Humanae_vitae"><i>Humanae vitae</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Humanae vitae">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Humanae_vitae" title="Humanae vitae">Humanae vitae</a></div>
<p>Of his seven encyclicals, Pope Paul VI is best known for his encyclical <i>Humanae vitae</i> (<i>Of Human Life</i>, subtitled <i>On the Regulation of Birth</i>), published on 25 July 1968. In this encyclical he reaffirmed the Catholic Church's traditional view of marriage and marital relations and its condemnation of artificial <a href="/wiki/Contraception" class="mw-redirect" title="Contraception">birth control</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> There were two Papal committees and numerous independent experts looking into the latest advancement of science and medicine on the question of artificial birth control.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> which were noted by the Pope in his encyclical<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> The expressed views of Paul VI reflected the teachings of his predecessors, especially <a href="/wiki/Pius_XI" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XI">Pius XI</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII">Pius XII</a><sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">John XXIII</a><sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> and never changed, as he repeatedly stated them in the first few years of his Pontificate.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>To the pope as to all his predecessors, marital relations are much more than a union of two people. They constitute a union of the loving couple with a loving God, in which the two persons create a new person materially, while God completes the creation by adding the soul. For this reason, Paul VI teaches in the first sentence of <i><a href="/wiki/Humanae_vitae" title="Humanae vitae">Humanae vitae</a></i> that the transmission of human life is a most serious role in which married people collaborate freely and responsibly with God the Creator.<sup id="cite_ref-Humanae_vitae,_1_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Humanae_vitae,_1-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> This divine partnership, according to Paul VI, does not allow for arbitrary human decisions, which may limit divine providence. The Pope does not paint an overly romantic picture of marriage: marital relations are a source of great joy, but also of difficulties and hardships.<sup id="cite_ref-Humanae_vitae,_1_125-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Humanae_vitae,_1-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> The question of human procreation exceeds in the view of Paul VI specific disciplines such as <a href="/wiki/Biology" title="Biology">biology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Psychology" title="Psychology">psychology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Demography" title="Demography">demography</a> or <a href="/wiki/Sociology" title="Sociology">sociology</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> The reason for this, according to Paul VI, is that married love takes its origin from God, who "is love". From this basic dignity, he defines his position:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Love is total—that very special form of personal friendship in which husband and wife generously share everything, allowing no unreasonable exceptions and not thinking solely of their own convenience. Whoever really loves his partner loves not only for what he receives, but loves that partner for the partner's own sake, content to be able to enrich the other with the gift of himself.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>The reaction to the encyclical's continued prohibitions of artificial birth control was very mixed. In Italy, Spain, Portugal and Poland, the encyclical was welcomed.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> In Latin America, much support developed for the Pope and his encyclical. As World Bank President <a href="/wiki/Robert_McNamara" title="Robert McNamara">Robert McNamara</a> declared at the 1968 <a href="/wiki/Annual_Meetings_of_the_International_Monetary_Fund_and_the_World_Bank_Group" title="Annual Meetings of the International Monetary Fund and the World Bank Group">Annual Meeting of the International Monetary Fund and the World Bank Group</a> that countries permitting birth control practices would get preferential access to resources, doctors in <a href="/wiki/La_Paz" title="La Paz">La Paz</a>, Bolivia called it insulting that money should be exchanged for the conscience of a Catholic nation. In Colombia, Cardinal archbishop <a href="/wiki/An%C3%ADbal_Mu%C3%B1oz_Duque" title="Aníbal Muñoz Duque">Aníbal Muñoz Duque</a> declared, "If American conditionality undermines Papal teachings, we prefer not to receive one cent".<sup id="cite_ref-Herder_549_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Herder_549-129">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The Senate of Bolivia passed a resolution stating that <i>Humanae vitae</i> could be discussed in its implications for individual consciences, but was of greatest significance because the papal document defended the rights of developing nations to determine their own population policies.<sup id="cite_ref-Herder_549_129-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Herder_549-129">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Jesuit" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesuit">Jesuit</a> Journal <i>Sic</i> dedicated one edition to the encyclical with supportive contributions.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Paul VI was concerned but not surprised by the negative reaction in Western Europe and the United States. He fully anticipated this reaction to be a temporary one: "Don't be afraid", he reportedly told <a href="/wiki/Edouard_Gagnon" class="mw-redirect" title="Edouard Gagnon">Edouard Gagnon</a> on the eve of the encyclical, "in twenty years time they'll call me a prophet."<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> His biography on the Vatican's website notes his reaffirmations of priestly celibacy and the traditional teaching on contraception that "[t]he controversies over these two pronouncements tended to overshadow the last years of his pontificate".<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> later reaffirmed and expanded upon <i>Humanae vitae</i> with the encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Evangelium_vitae" title="Evangelium vitae">Evangelium vitae</a></i>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Evangelism">Evangelism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Evangelism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>By taking the name of Paul, the newly elected Pope showed his intention to take the <a href="/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle" title="Paul the Apostle">Apostle Paul</a> as a model for his papal ministry.<sup id="cite_ref-DullesChurchSociety_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DullesChurchSociety-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> In 1967, when he reorganised the Roman curia, Pope Paul renamed the Congregation for the Propagation of the Faith as the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Evangelization_of_Peoples" title="Congregation for the Evangelization of Peoples">Congregation for the Evangelization of Peoples</a>. Pope Paul was the first pope in history to make apostolic journeys to other continents and visited six continents.<sup id="cite_ref-DullesChurchSociety_133-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DullesChurchSociety-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> The Pope chose the theme of <a href="/wiki/Evangelism" title="Evangelism">evangelism</a> for the synod of bishops in 1974. From materials generated by that synod, he composed the 1975 apostolic exhortation on evangelisation, <a href="/wiki/Evangelii_nuntiandi" title="Evangelii nuntiandi">Evangelii nuntiandi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DullesChurchSociety_133-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DullesChurchSociety-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ecumenism_and_ecumenical_relations">Ecumenism and ecumenical relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Ecumenism and ecumenical relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>After the council, Paul VI contributed in two ways to the continued growth of ecumenical dialogue. The <a href="/wiki/Separated_brethren" title="Separated brethren">separated brothers and sisters</a>, as he called them, were not able to contribute to the council as invited observers. After the council, many of them took initiative to seek out their Catholic counterparts and the Pope in Rome, who welcomed such visits. But the Catholic Church itself recognised from the many previous ecumenical encounters, that much needed to be done within, to be an open partner for ecumenism.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidt811–12_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidt811–12-134">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> To those who are entrusted the highest and deepest truth and therefore, so Paul VI, believed that he had the most difficult part to communicate. Ecumenical dialogue, in the view of Paul VI, requires from a Catholic the whole person: one's entire reason, will, and heart.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967177_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967177-135">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> Paul VI, like Pius XII before him, was reluctant to give in on a lowest possible point. And yet, Paul felt compelled to admit his ardent Gospel-based desire to be everything to everybody and to help all people<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967181_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967181-136">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Being the successor of Peter, he felt the words of Christ, "Do you love me more" like a sharp knife penetrating to the marrow of his soul. These words meant to Paul VI love without limits,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967185_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967185-137">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> and they underscore the church's fundamental approach to ecumenism.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Orthodox">Orthodox</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Orthodox">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Paul VI visited the Orthodox Patriarchs of Jerusalem and Constantinople in 1964 and 1967. He was the first pope since the ninth century to visit the East, labelling the Eastern Churches as sister churches.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_429_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_429-138">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> He was also the first pope in centuries to meet the heads of various <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox</a> faiths. Notably, his meeting with <a href="/wiki/Ecumenical_Patriarch_Athenagoras_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Ecumenical Patriarch Athenagoras I">Ecumenical Patriarch Athenagoras I</a> in 1964 in <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a> led to rescinding the <a href="/wiki/Excommunication" title="Excommunication">excommunications</a> of the <a href="/wiki/East-West_Schism" class="mw-redirect" title="East-West Schism">Great Schism</a>, which took place in 1054.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This was a significant step towards restoring communion between Rome and Constantinople. It produced the <a href="/wiki/Catholic-Orthodox_Joint_declaration_of_1965" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic-Orthodox Joint declaration of 1965">Catholic-Orthodox Joint declaration of 1965</a>, which was read out on 7 December 1965, simultaneously at a public meeting of the Second Vatican Council in Rome and at a special ceremony in Istanbul. The declaration did not end the schism, but showed a desire for greater reconciliation between the two churches.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_429_138-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_429-138">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> In May 1973, the Coptic Patriarch <a href="/wiki/Shenouda_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Shenouda III">Shenouda III</a> of Alexandria visited the Vatican, where he met three times with Pope Paul VI. A common declaration and a joint <a href="/wiki/Creed" title="Creed">Creed</a> issued after the visit proclaimed unity in a number of theological issues,<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_430_111-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_430-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> though also that other theological differences "since the year 451" "cannot be ignored" while both traditions work to a greater unity.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Anglicans">Anglicans</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Anglicans">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Paul VI was the first pope to receive an <a href="/wiki/Anglican" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglican">Anglican</a> <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Canterbury" title="Archbishop of Canterbury">Archbishop of Canterbury</a>, <a href="/wiki/Michael_Ramsey" title="Michael Ramsey">Michael Ramsey</a>, in official audience as Head of Church, after the private audience visit of Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Geoffrey_Fisher" title="Geoffrey Fisher">Geoffrey Fisher</a> to <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Pope John XXIII</a> on 2 December 1960.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967198_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967198-141">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Ramsey met Paul three times during his visit and opened the Anglican Centre in Rome to increase their mutual knowledge.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidt_813_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidt_813-142">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> He praised Paul VI<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;d&#93;</a></sup> and his contributions in the service of unity.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidt_813_142-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidt_813-142">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> Paul replied that "by entering into our house, you are entering your own house, we are happy to open our door and heart to you."<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidt_813_142-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidt_813-142">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> The two church leaders signed a common declaration, which put an end to the disputes of the past and outlined a common agenda for the future.
</p><p>Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Augustin_Bea" title="Augustin Bea">Augustin Bea</a>, the head of the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Council_for_Promoting_Christian_Unity" class="mw-redirect" title="Pontifical Council for Promoting Christian Unity">Secretariat for Promoting Christian Unity</a>, added at the end of the visit, "Let us move forward in Christ. God wants it. Humanity is waiting for it."<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> Unmoved by a harsh condemnation by the Congregation of Faith on mixed marriages precisely at this time of the visit, Paul VI and Ramsey appointed a preparatory commission which was to put the common agenda into practice on such issues as mixed marriages. This resulted in a joint Malta declaration, the first joint agreement on the <a href="/wiki/Creed" title="Creed">Creed</a> since the <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Reformation" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant Reformation">Reformation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> Paul VI was a good friend of the Anglican Church, which he described as "our beloved sister Church". This description was unique to Paul and not used by later popes.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Protestants">Protestants</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Protestants">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 1965, Paul VI decided on the creation of a joint working group with the <a href="/wiki/World_Council_of_Churches" title="World Council of Churches">World Council of Churches</a> to map all possible avenues of dialogue and co-operation. In the following three years, eight sessions were held which resulted in many joint proposals.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> It was proposed to work closely together in areas of social justice and development and Third World Issues such as hunger and poverty. On the religious side, it was agreed to share together in the <a href="/wiki/Week_of_Prayer_for_Christian_Unity" title="Week of Prayer for Christian Unity">Week of Prayer for Christian Unity</a>, to be held every year. The joint working group was to prepare texts which were to be used by all Christians.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> On 19 July 1968, the meeting of the World Council of Churches took place in <a href="/wiki/Uppsala" title="Uppsala">Uppsala</a>, Sweden, which Pope Paul called a sign of the times. He sent his blessing in an ecumenical manner: "May the Lord bless everything you do for the case of Christian Unity."<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> The World Council of Churches decided on including Catholic theologians in its committees, provided they have the backing of the Vatican.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Lutheran" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran">Lutherans</a> were the first Protestant church offering a dialogue to the Catholic Church in September 1964 in <a href="/wiki/Reykjav%C3%ADk" title="Reykjavík">Reykjavík</a>, Iceland.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> It resulted in joint study groups of several issues. The dialogue with the <a href="/wiki/Methodist_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Methodist Church">Methodist Church</a> began October 1965, after its representatives officially applauded remarkable changes, friendship and co-operation of the past five years. The <a href="/wiki/Reformed_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Reformed Church">Reformed Churches</a> entered four years later into a dialogue with the Catholic Church.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The President of the <a href="/wiki/Lutheran_World_Federation" title="Lutheran World Federation">Lutheran World Federation</a> and member of the central committee of the World Council of Churches <a href="/wiki/Fredrik_A._Schiotz" title="Fredrik A. Schiotz">Fredrik A. Schiotz</a> stated during the 450th anniversary of the <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Reformation" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant Reformation">Reformation</a>, that earlier commemorations were viewed almost as a triumph. Reformation should be celebrated as a thanksgiving to God, his truth and his renewed life. He welcomed the announcement of Pope Paul VI to celebrate the 1900th anniversary of the death of the <a href="/wiki/Apostle_Peter" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostle Peter">Apostle Peter</a> and <a href="/wiki/Apostle_Paul" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostle Paul">Apostle Paul</a>, and promised the participation and co-operation in the festivities.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Paul VI supported the new-found harmony and co-operation with Protestants on many levels. When Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Augustin_Bea" title="Augustin Bea">Augustin Bea</a> went to see him for permission for a joint Catholic-Protestant translation of the Bible with Protestant Bible societies, the pope walked towards him and exclaimed, "as far as the cooperation with <a href="/wiki/Bible_society" title="Bible society">Bible societies</a> is concerned, I am totally in favour."<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> He issued a formal approval on <a href="/wiki/Pentecost" title="Pentecost">Pentecost</a> 1967, the feast on which the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Spirit" title="Holy Spirit">Holy Spirit</a> descended on the Christians, overcoming all linguistic difficulties, according to Christian tradition.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatifications_and_canonisations">Beatifications and canonisations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Beatifications and canonisations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/List_of_people_declared_venerable_by_Pope_Paul_VI" title="List of people declared venerable by Pope Paul VI">List of people declared venerable by Pope Paul VI</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_people_beatified_by_Pope_Paul_VI" title="List of people beatified by Pope Paul VI">List of people beatified by Pope Paul VI</a>, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_saints_canonized_by_Pope_Paul_VI" title="List of saints canonized by Pope Paul VI">List of saints canonized by Pope Paul VI</a></div>
<p>Paul VI beatified a total of 38 individuals in his pontificate and he canonised 84 saints in 21 causes. Among the beatifications included <a href="/wiki/Maximilian_Kolbe" title="Maximilian Kolbe">Maximilian Kolbe</a> (1971) and the <a href="/wiki/Korean_Martyrs" title="Korean Martyrs">Korean Martyrs</a> (1968). He canonised saints such as <a href="/wiki/Nikola_Taveli%C4%87" class="mw-redirect" title="Nikola Tavelić">Nikola Tavelić</a> (1970) and the <a href="/wiki/Ugandan_Martyrs" class="mw-redirect" title="Ugandan Martyrs">Ugandan Martyrs</a> (1964).
</p><p>Paul VI also named two <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_the_Church" title="Doctor of the Church">Doctors of the Church</a> and in so doing named the first two female Doctors of the Church. He named <a href="/wiki/Teresa_of_%C3%81vila" title="Teresa of Ávila">Teresa of Ávila</a> (he titled her as "<i>Doctor orationis</i>" or "Doctor of Prayer") on 27 September 1970 and <a href="/wiki/Catherine_of_Siena" title="Catherine of Siena">Catherine of Siena</a> on 4 October 1970.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Consistories">Consistories</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Consistories">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Paul_VI" title="Cardinals created by Paul VI">Cardinals created by Paul VI</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Karol_Wojtyla%27s_cardinal_proclamation.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Karol_Wojtyla%27s_cardinal_proclamation.jpg/200px-Karol_Wojtyla%27s_cardinal_proclamation.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="145" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Karol_Wojtyla%27s_cardinal_proclamation.jpg/300px-Karol_Wojtyla%27s_cardinal_proclamation.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Karol_Wojtyla%27s_cardinal_proclamation.jpg/400px-Karol_Wojtyla%27s_cardinal_proclamation.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1240" data-file-height="900" /></a><figcaption>Paul VI makes Karol Wojtyła (future <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>) a cardinal in 1967.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paulo_VI_e_Ratzinger.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Paulo_VI_e_Ratzinger.jpg/200px-Paulo_VI_e_Ratzinger.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="134" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Paulo_VI_e_Ratzinger.jpg/300px-Paulo_VI_e_Ratzinger.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Paulo_VI_e_Ratzinger.jpg/400px-Paulo_VI_e_Ratzinger.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1072" /></a><figcaption>Paul VI with Joseph Ratzinger (future <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a>) who was created cardinal in the 1977 consistory.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Pope Paul VI held six <a href="/wiki/Papal_consistory" title="Papal consistory">consistories</a> between 1965 and 1977 that raised 143 men to the <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinalate</a> in his fifteen years as pope:
</p>
<ul><li>22 February 1965, 27 cardinals</li>
<li>26 June 1967, 27 cardinals</li>
<li>28 April 1969, 34 cardinals</li>
<li>5 March 1973, 30 cardinals</li>
<li>24 May 1976, 20 cardinals</li>
<li>27 June 1977, 4 cardinals</li></ul>
<p>The next three popes were created <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinals</a> by him. His immediate successor, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I" title="Pope John Paul I">Albino Luciani</a>, who took the name John Paul I, was created a cardinal in the consistory of 5 March 1973. <a href="/wiki/Karol_J%C3%B3zef_Wojty%C5%82a" class="mw-redirect" title="Karol Józef Wojtyła">Karol Józef Wojtyła</a> (later Pope John Paul II) was created a cardinal in the consistory of 26 June 1967. <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Ratzinger" class="mw-redirect" title="Joseph Ratzinger">Joseph Ratzinger</a> (later Pope Benedict XVI) was created a cardinal in the small four-appointment consistory of 27 June 1977 that was the pope's last.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993669_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993669-154">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> Paul VI named <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0t%C4%9Bp%C3%A1n_Trochta" title="Štěpán Trochta">Štěpán Trochta</a> and <a href="/wiki/Iuliu_Hossu" title="Iuliu Hossu">Iuliu Hossu</a> as cardinals "<i><a href="/wiki/In_pectore" title="In pectore">in pectore</a></i>" in 1969 and only revealed Hossu's name in 1973 after Hossu died while formally naming Trochta. Similarly, Paul VI named both <a href="/wiki/Franti%C5%A1ek_Tom%C3%A1%C5%A1ek" title="František Tomášek">František Tomášek</a> and <a href="/wiki/Joseph-Marie_Tr%E1%BB%8Bnh_Nh%C6%B0_Khu%C3%AA" title="Joseph-Marie Trịnh Như Khuê">Joseph-Marie Trịnh Như Khuê</a> "<i>in pectore</i>" in 1976, only announcing the former in 1977 and the latter at the 1976 consistory itself, a month after having announced it and his hidden selection.
</p><p>With the six consistories, Paul VI continued the internationalisation policies started by Pius XII in 1946 and continued by John XXIII. In his 1976 consistory, five of twenty cardinals originated from Africa, one of them a son of a tribal chief with fifty wives.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993669_154-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993669-154">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> Several prominent Latin Americans like <a href="/wiki/Eduardo_Francisco_Pironio" title="Eduardo Francisco Pironio">Eduardo Francisco Pironio</a> of Argentina; <a href="/wiki/Luis_Aponte_Martinez" class="mw-redirect" title="Luis Aponte Martinez">Luis Aponte Martinez</a> of <a href="/wiki/Puerto_Rico" title="Puerto Rico">Puerto Rico</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eug%C3%AAnio_de_Ara%C3%BAjo_Sales" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugênio de Araújo Sales">Eugênio de Araújo Sales</a> and <a href="/wiki/Aloisio_Lorscheider" class="mw-redirect" title="Aloisio Lorscheider">Aloisio Lorscheider</a> from Brazil were also elevated by him. There were voices within the church at the time saying that the European period of the church was coming to a close, a view shared by Britain's Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Basil_Hume" title="Basil Hume">Basil Hume</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993669_154-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993669-154">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> At the same time, the members of the College of Cardinals lost some of their previous influences, after Paul VI decreed, that membership by bishops in committees and other bodies of the Roman Curia would not be limited to cardinals. The age limit of eighty years imposed by the Pope, a numerical increase of Cardinals by almost 100%, and a reform of the formal dress of the "Princes of the Church" further contributed to a service-oriented perception of Cardinals under his pontificate. The increased number of Cardinals from the Third World and the papal emphasis on related issues was nevertheless welcomed by many in Western Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993669_154-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993669-154">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> The consistory of 1969 was the largest consistory since 1946 and would be surpassed later in 2001.
</p><p>In 1965, the theologian <a href="/wiki/Romano_Guardini" title="Romano Guardini">Romano Guardini</a> declined an invitation by Paul VI to be inducted into the College of Cardinals. In 1967, he also intended to nominate <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Sigismondi" title="Pietro Sigismondi">Pietro Sigismondi</a>, but he died a month before the consistory was held. Also in 1967, according to the memoirs of <a href="/wiki/Louis_Bouyer" title="Louis Bouyer">Louis Bouyer</a>, Paul VI intended to name Bouyer to the cardinalate after the Second Vatican Council, however, Paul VI was forced to abandon the idea after realizing that the appointment would not be warmly received by the French episcopacy since Bouyer had been very critical of many of the positions taken by the French bishops. Other sources indicate that the pope intended to name his friend <a href="/wiki/Jacques_Maritain" title="Jacques Maritain">Jacques Maritain</a> to the cardinalate in 1969. Not only did Maritain decline, but if he was elevated it would have made him the first <a href="/wiki/Lay_cardinal" title="Lay cardinal">lay cardinal</a> since 1858.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> On 22 February 1969, Paul VI and Monsignor Hieronymus Menges discussed nominating Iuliu Hossu and <a href="/wiki/%C3%81ron_M%C3%A1rton" title="Áron Márton">Áron Márton</a> to the cardinalate (Pius XII dropped an idea to name Márton to the cardinalate in 1946), however, Márton's potential elevation was not considered acceptable, hence, Hossu was named <i>in pectore</i> since the Romanians would not have accepted Hossu either.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Final_years_and_death">Final years and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Final years and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Rumours_of_homosexuality_and_denial">Rumours of homosexuality and denial</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Rumours of homosexuality and denial">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1976 Paul VI became the first pontiff in the modern era to deny the accusation of <a href="/wiki/Homosexuality_and_Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Homosexuality and Catholicism">homosexuality</a>. On 29 December 1975, the Sacred Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith issued a document entitled <i>Persona Humana: Declaration on Certain Questions concerning Sexual Ethics</i>, that reaffirmed church teaching that pre- or extramarital sex, homosexual activity, and masturbation are sinful acts.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> In response, <a href="/wiki/Roger_Peyrefitte" title="Roger Peyrefitte">Roger Peyrefitte</a>, who had already written in two of his books that Paul VI had a longtime homosexual relationship, repeated his charges in a magazine interview with a French gay magazine that, when reprinted in Italian, brought the rumours to a wider public and caused an uproar. He said that the pope was a hypocrite who had a longtime sexual relationship with an actor.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Observer_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Observer-160">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> Widespread rumours identified the actor as <a href="/wiki/Paolo_Carlini" title="Paolo Carlini">Paolo Carlini</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Posner_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Posner-162">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> who had a small part in the <a href="/wiki/Audrey_Hepburn" title="Audrey Hepburn">Audrey Hepburn</a> film <i><a href="/wiki/Roman_Holiday" title="Roman Holiday">Roman Holiday</a></i> (1953). In a brief address to a crowd of approximately 20,000 in St Peter's Square on 18 April, Paul VI called the charges "horrible and slanderous insinuations" and appealed for prayers on his behalf. Special prayers for the pope were said in all Italian Catholic churches in "a day of consolation".<sup id="cite_ref-Observer_160-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Observer-160">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Posner_162-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Posner-162">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;e&#93;</a></sup> The charges have resurfaced periodically. In 1994, Franco Bellegrandi, a former Vatican honour chamberlain and correspondent for the Vatican newspaper <i><a href="/wiki/L%27Osservatore_Romano" title="L&#39;Osservatore Romano">L'Osservatore Romano</a></i>, alleged that Paul VI had been blackmailed and had promoted other gay men to positions of power within the Vatican.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> In 2006, the newspaper <i>L'Espresso</i> reported that the private papers of police commander General Giorgio Manes accepted the blackmail story as true, and that they claimed Italian Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Aldo_Moro" title="Aldo Moro">Aldo Moro</a> had been asked to help.<sup id="cite_ref-Posner_162-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Posner-162">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paolo_VI_e_Luciani.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Paolo_VI_e_Luciani.jpg/185px-Paolo_VI_e_Luciani.jpg" decoding="async" width="185" height="244" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Paolo_VI_e_Luciani.jpg/278px-Paolo_VI_e_Luciani.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Paolo_VI_e_Luciani.jpg/370px-Paolo_VI_e_Luciani.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1840" data-file-height="2423" /></a><figcaption>Paul VI with <a href="/wiki/Albino_Luciani" class="mw-redirect" title="Albino Luciani">Albino Luciani</a> (later John Paul I) in <a href="/wiki/Venice" title="Venice">Venice</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Paul VI had been in good health prior to his pontifical election. His health following his papal election took a turn when he needed to undergo a serious operation to treat an enlarged <a href="/wiki/Prostate" title="Prostate">prostate</a>. The pope procrastinated in this but relented in November 1967; the operation took place on a simple table in an improvised operating theatre in the papal apartments by a team led by Professor Pietro Valdoni. The Vatican was delicate in their description of what the pope underwent and referred to it as "the malaise from which the Holy Father had been suffering for weeks". As a result of the delay in having the operation, the pope had to wear a <a href="/wiki/Catheter" title="Catheter">catheter</a> for a period following the operation and still was by December.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The pope discussed business from his bed about 48 hours after the operation with Cardinal Amleto Cicognani and at that point was off <a href="/wiki/Intravenous_feeding" class="mw-redirect" title="Intravenous feeding">intravenous feeding</a> in favour of orange juice and hot broth. Cardinal Cicognani said the pope was "in good general condition" and that he spoke in a "clear and firm voice". The pope's two brothers also visited him at his bedside following a "tranquil night" for the pope. The doctors also reported the pope's condition to have been "excellent".<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Kidnapping_and_death_of_Aldo_Moro">Kidnapping and death of Aldo Moro</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: Kidnapping and death of Aldo Moro">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Aldo_Moro_br.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Aldo_Moro_br.jpg/220px-Aldo_Moro_br.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="255" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Aldo_Moro_br.jpg/330px-Aldo_Moro_br.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Aldo_Moro_br.jpg/440px-Aldo_Moro_br.jpg 2x" data-file-width="737" data-file-height="853" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Aldo_Moro" title="Aldo Moro">Aldo Moro</a>, photographed during his kidnapping by the <a href="/wiki/Red_Brigades" title="Red Brigades">Red Brigades</a> in 1978</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 16 March 1978, former <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Italy" title="Prime Minister of Italy">Italian prime minister</a> <a href="/wiki/Aldo_Moro" title="Aldo Moro">Aldo Moro</a>—a friend of Paul VI's from his FUCI student days—was kidnapped by a far-left Italian terrorist group known as the <a href="/wiki/Red_Brigades" title="Red Brigades">Red Brigades</a>. The kidnapping kept the world and the pope in suspense for 55 days.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> On 20 April, Moro directly appealed to the pope to intervene as <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> had intervened in the case of Professor <a href="/wiki/Giuliano_Vassalli" title="Giuliano Vassalli">Giuliano Vassalli</a> in the same situation.<sup id="cite_ref-Hebblethwaite_700-701_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hebblethwaite_700-701-170">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> The eighty-year-old Paul VI wrote a letter to the <a href="/wiki/Red_Brigades" title="Red Brigades">Red Brigades</a>:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>I have no mandate to speak to you, and I am not bound by any private interests in his regard. But I love him as a member of the great human family as a friend of student days and by a very special title as a brother in faith and as a son of the Church of Christ. I make an appeal that you will certainly not ignore. On my knees I beg you, free Aldo Moro, simply without conditions, not so much because of my humble and well-meaning intercession, but because he shares with you the common dignity of a brother in humanity. Men of the Red Brigades, leave me, the interpreter of the voices of so many of our fellow citizens, the hope that in your heart feelings of humanity will triumph. In prayer and always loving you I await proof of that.<sup id="cite_ref-Hebblethwaite_700-701_170-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hebblethwaite_700-701-170">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Some in the Italian government accused the pope of treating the Red Brigades too kindly. Paul VI continued looking for ways to pay ransom for Moro, but his efforts were fruitless. On 9 May, the bullet-riddled body of Aldo Moro was found in a car in Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Pope Paul VI later celebrated his State Funeral Mass.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Final_days">Final days</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: Final days">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Basilica_National_Shrine_Immaculate_Conception_DC_25.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Basilica_National_Shrine_Immaculate_Conception_DC_25.JPG/220px-Basilica_National_Shrine_Immaculate_Conception_DC_25.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Basilica_National_Shrine_Immaculate_Conception_DC_25.JPG/330px-Basilica_National_Shrine_Immaculate_Conception_DC_25.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Basilica_National_Shrine_Immaculate_Conception_DC_25.JPG/440px-Basilica_National_Shrine_Immaculate_Conception_DC_25.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2736" data-file-height="3648" /></a><figcaption>Paul VI's <a href="/wiki/List_of_papal_tiaras_in_existence" title="List of papal tiaras in existence">papal tiara</a>, now in the Crypt of the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Immaculate_Conception" title="Basilica of the National Shrine of the Immaculate Conception">Basilica of the National Shrine of the Immaculate Conception</a></figcaption></figure><p>Pope Paul VI left the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Palace" title="Apostolic Palace">Vatican</a> to go to the papal summer residence, Castel Gandolfo, on 14 July 1978, visiting on the way the tomb of Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Pizzardo" title="Giuseppe Pizzardo">Giuseppe Pizzardo</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Hebblethwaite_707_172-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hebblethwaite_707-172">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> who had introduced him to the Vatican half a century earlier. Although he was sick, he agreed to see the new Italian President <a href="/wiki/Sandro_Pertini" title="Sandro Pertini">Sandro Pertini</a> for over two hours. In the evening he watched a <a href="/wiki/Western_(genre)" title="Western (genre)">Western</a> on television, happy only when he saw "horses, the most beautiful animals that God had created."<sup id="cite_ref-Hebblethwaite_707_172-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hebblethwaite_707-172">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> He had breathing problems and needed oxygen. On Sunday, at the Feast of the Transfiguration, he was tired, but wanted to say the <a href="/wiki/Angelus" title="Angelus">Angelus</a>. He was neither able nor permitted to do so and instead stayed in bed, his temperature rising.
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=50" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Negativos_2934.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Negativos_2934.jpg/220px-Negativos_2934.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Negativos_2934.jpg/330px-Negativos_2934.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Negativos_2934.jpg/440px-Negativos_2934.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6188" data-file-height="4082" /></a><figcaption>Paul VI's body in the Vatican after his death</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:SanctusPaulusVI.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/SanctusPaulusVI.jpg/220px-SanctusPaulusVI.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/SanctusPaulusVI.jpg/330px-SanctusPaulusVI.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/SanctusPaulusVI.jpg/440px-SanctusPaulusVI.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3024" data-file-height="4032" /></a><figcaption>Tomb of Paul VI following his canonisation in October 2018</figcaption></figure>
<p>From his bed he participated in Sunday Mass at 18:00. After receiving communion, the pope suffered a massive heart attack, after which he lived on for three more hours. On 6 August 1978 at 21:41, Paul VI died in Castel Gandolfo.<sup id="cite_ref-Hebblethwaite_707_172-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hebblethwaite_707-172">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Before he died, the pontiff had been lucid after the first heart attack but an hour before his death felt dizzy and asked those present to continue the prayers in his stead. Present at his bedside at the time of his death were Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Jean-Marie_Villot" title="Jean-Marie Villot">Jean-Marie Villot</a>, Bishop <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Caprio" title="Giuseppe Caprio">Giuseppe Caprio</a>, and his personal secretary Pasquale Macchi, as well as two nuns and his personal physician. By the time the pope died, he was already confined to bed due to a flare up in his chronic joint arthritis and could not get up to personally celebrate the Mass.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Upon the initial heart attack, the pope was immediately given oxygen; however, the Holy See indicated that his heart condition was aggravated by a <a href="/wiki/Pulmonary_edema" title="Pulmonary edema">pulmonary edema</a>, or the seeping of fluid into the lungs. 
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a><sup id="cite_ref-larouchepub.com_174-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-larouchepub.com-174">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> declared nine days of mourning; <a href="/wiki/Egypt" title="Egypt">Egypt</a><sup id="cite_ref-larouchepub.com_174-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-larouchepub.com-174">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> declared seven days of mourning; <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a><sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> declared four days of mourning; <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-larouchepub.com_174-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-larouchepub.com-174">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a><sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Zaire" title="Zaire">Zaire</a><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> declared three days of mourning; <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a><sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> declared one day of mourning.
</p><p>Paul VI left a will and a spiritual testament.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> Those were released by the Vatican shortly after his death. In it, he asked for his burial to be simple, and that his correspondence, personal memos and other writings be destroyed. He also legated all his belongings to the Vatican.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Paul VI does not have an ornate <a href="/wiki/Sarcophagus" title="Sarcophagus">sarcophagus</a>, but is buried in a grave beneath the floor of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a>, though in an area of the basilica's crypt near the tombs of other popes.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag failed verification of its source citation(s). (September 2019)">failed verification</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>
His position mirrors the statements attributed to <a href="/wiki/Pius_XI" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XI">Pius XI</a>: "a Pope may suffer but he must be able to function" and by Pius XII.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> Pope Paul, reflecting on <a href="/wiki/Hamlet" title="Hamlet">Hamlet</a>, wrote the following in a private note in 1978:<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"></p><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>What is my state of mind? Am I Hamlet? Or <a href="/wiki/Don_Quixote" title="Don Quixote">Don Quixote</a>? On the left? On the right? I do not think I have been properly understood. I am filled with 'great joy (Superabundo gaudio)' With all our affliction, I am overjoyed (2 Cor 2:4).<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>His confessor, the <a href="/wiki/Jesuit" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesuit">Jesuit</a> <a href="/wiki/Paolo_Dezza" title="Paolo Dezza">Paolo Dezza</a>, said that "this pope is a man of great joy",<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993339_68-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993339-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> and that:
</p>
<blockquote><p>If Paul VI was not a saint, when he was elected Pope, he became one during his pontificate.
