I was able to witness not only with what energy and dedication he toiled for Christ and the Church but also and above all, how much he suffered for Christ and the Church. I always admired not only his deep inner resignation but also his constant abandonment to divine providence.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993600_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHebblethwaite1993600-184">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> </p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonization">Canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=51" title="Edit section: Canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Beatification_and_canonization_of_Pope_Paul_VI" title="Beatification and canonization of Pope Paul VI">Beatification and canonization of Pope Paul VI</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;">Pope <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">Saint</a></div><br /><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">Paul VI</div><br /><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Rome" class="mw-redirect" title="Patriarch of Rome">Patriarch of Rome</a></div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><span class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Frameless"><a href="/wiki/File:Papa_Paolo_VI.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Papa_Paolo_VI.jpg/220px-Papa_Paolo_VI.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="287" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Papa_Paolo_VI.jpg/330px-Papa_Paolo_VI.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Papa_Paolo_VI.jpg/440px-Papa_Paolo_VI.jpg 2x" data-file-width="829" data-file-height="1080" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color:gold;"><a href="/wiki/Pope" title="Pope">Pope</a>; <a href="/wiki/Confessor" title="Confessor">Confessor</a></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data">Giovanni Battista Enrico Antonio Maria Montini<br />26 September 1897<br /><a href="/wiki/Concesio" title="Concesio">Concesio</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brescia" title="Brescia">Brescia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Kingdom of Italy</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">6 August 1978 (aged 80)<br /><a href="/wiki/Castel_Gandolfo" title="Castel Gandolfo">Castel Gandolfo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italian Republic</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Beatified</a></th><td class="infobox-data">19 October 2014, <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Square" title="St. Peter&#39;s Square">St. Peter's Square</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">Canonized</a></th><td class="infobox-data">14 October 2018, St. Peter's Square, Vatican City by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">Feast</a></th><td class="infobox-data">29 May</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Papal_regalia_and_insignia" title="Papal regalia and insignia">Papal vestments</a>, <a href="/wiki/Papal_tiara" title="Papal tiara">Papal tiara</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pallium" title="Pallium">Pallium</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data">Archdiocese of Milan, Paul VI Pontifical Institute, Second Vatican Council, Diocese of Brescia, Concesio, Magenta, Paderno Dugnano</td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Beatification_of_Paul_VI.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Beatification_of_Paul_VI.jpg/220px-Beatification_of_Paul_VI.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="391" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Beatification_of_Paul_VI.jpg/330px-Beatification_of_Paul_VI.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Beatification_of_Paul_VI.jpg/440px-Beatification_of_Paul_VI.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1836" data-file-height="3264" /></a><figcaption>Tapestry of Paul VI on the occasion of his beatification on 19 October 2014</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._(44398703435).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg/220px-Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg/330px-Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg/440px-Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6720" data-file-height="4480" /></a><figcaption>Canonization Mass held on 14 October 2018</figcaption></figure>
<p>The diocesan process for beatification for Paul VI—titled then as a <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>—opened in Rome on 11 May 1993 under <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> after the "<a href="/wiki/Nihil_obstat" title="Nihil obstat">nihil obstat</a>" ("nothing against") was declared the previous 18 March. Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Camillo_Ruini" title="Camillo Ruini">Camillo Ruini</a> opened the diocesan process in Rome. The title of Servant of God is the first of four steps toward possible <a href="/wiki/Canonisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonisation">canonisation</a>. The diocesan process concluded its business on 18 March 1998.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 20 December 2012, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a>, in an audience with the Cardinal Prefect of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a> <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Amato" title="Angelo Amato">Angelo Amato</a>, declared that the late pontiff had lived a life of <a href="/wiki/Heroic_virtue" title="Heroic virtue">heroic virtue</a>, which means that he could be called "<a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 12 December 2013, Vatican officials comprising a medical panel approved a supposed miracle that was attributed to the intercession of the late pontiff, which was the curing of an unborn child in <a href="/wiki/California" title="California">California</a>, U.S.A in the 1990s. This miracle was investigated in California from 7 July 2003 until 12 July 2004. It was expected that <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> would approve the miracle in the near future, thus, warranting the beatification of the late pontiff.<sup id="cite_ref-uscatholic_187-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-uscatholic-187">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> In February 2014, it was reported that the consulting Vatican theologians to the Congregation for the Causes of Saints recognised the miracle attributed to the late pontiff on 18 February.<sup id="cite_ref-catholicnewsagency_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholicnewsagency-188">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> On 24 April 2014, it was reported in the Italian magazine <i>Credere</i> that the late pope could possibly be beatified on 19 October 2014. This report from the magazine further stated that several cardinals and bishops would meet on 5 May to confirm the miracle that had previously been approved, and then present it to Pope Francis who may sign the decree for beatification shortly after that.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> The Congregation for the Causes of Saints' cardinal and bishop members held that meeting and positively concluded that the healing was indeed a miracle that could be attributed to the late pope. The matter would then be presented by the Cardinal Prefect to the pope for approval.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The second miracle required for his canonisation was reported to have occurred in 2014 not long after his beatification occurred. The vice-postulator Antonio Lanzoni suggested that the canonisation could have been approved in the near future which would allow for the canonisation sometime in spring 2016; this did not materialise because the investigations were still ongoing at that stage.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> It was further reported in January 2017 that Pope Francis was considering canonising Paul VI either in that year, or in 2018 (marking 40 years since the late pope's death), without the second miracle required for sainthood.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> This too was proven false since the miracle from 2014 was being presented to the competent Vatican officials for assessment. His liturgical feast day is celebrated on the date of his birth, 26 September, rather than the day of his death as is usual since the latter falls on the Feast of the Transfiguration.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The final miracle needed for the late pope's canonisation was investigated in <a href="/wiki/Verona" title="Verona">Verona</a> and was closed on 11 March 2017. The miracle in question involves the healing of an unborn girl, Amanda Maria Paola (born 25 December 2014), after her parents (Vanna and Alberto) went to the <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_delle_Grazie,_Brescia" title="Santa Maria delle Grazie, Brescia">Santuario delle Grazie</a> in <a href="/wiki/Brescia" title="Brescia">Brescia</a> to pray for the late pope's intercession the previous 29 October, just ten days after Paul VI was beatified.<sup id="cite_ref-VPP_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VPP-196">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> The miracle regarding Amanda was the fact that she had survived for months despite the fact that the <a href="/wiki/Placenta" title="Placenta">placenta</a> was broken. On 23 September, a month before the beatification, Amanda's mother Vanna Pironato (aged 35) was hospitalised due to the premature rupture of the placenta, with doctors declaring her pregnancy to be at great risk.<sup id="cite_ref-VPP_196-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VPP-196">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> The documents regarding the alleged miracle are now in Rome awaiting approval; he shall be canonised should this healing be approved.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Theologians advising the Congregation for the Causes of Saints voiced their approval to this miracle on 13 December 2017 (following the confirmation of doctors on 26 October) and have this direction on to the cardinal and bishop members of the C.C.S. who must vote on the cause also before taking it to Pope Francis for his approval. Brescian media reports the canonisation could take place in October 2018 to coincide with <a href="/wiki/Fifteenth_Ordinary_General_Assembly_of_the_Synod_of_Bishops" title="Fifteenth Ordinary General Assembly of the Synod of Bishops">the synod on the youth</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-VPP_196-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VPP-196">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> The cardinal and bishop members of the C.C.S. issued their unanimous approval to this miracle in their meeting held on 6 February 2018. Pope Francis confirmed that the canonisation would be approved and celebrated in 2018 in remarks made during a meeting with Roman priests on 14 February 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> On 6 March 2018, the Cardinal Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Parolin" title="Pietro Parolin">Pietro Parolin</a>, speaking at a plenary meeting of the International Catholic Migration Commission in Rome, confirmed that Paul VI would be canonised in at the close of the synod on 28 October 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> On 6 March, the pope confirmed the healing as a miracle, thereby approving Paul VI's canonisation; a consistory of cardinals on 19 May 2018 determined that the official date for Paul VI's canonisation, along with that of the assassinated Archbishop of <a href="/wiki/Archdiocese_of_San_Salvador" class="mw-redirect" title="Archdiocese of San Salvador">San Salvador</a>, <a href="/wiki/%C3%93scar_Romero" title="Óscar Romero">Oscar Romero</a>, would be 14 October 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Paul VI's liturgical <a href="/wiki/Feast_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Feast day">feast day</a> was originally celebrated on 26 September, the date of his birth, but was moved to 29 May, the day of his priestly ordination, in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-feastday_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-feastday-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy_and_controversies">Legacy and controversies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Paul_VI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=52" title="Edit section: Legacy and controversies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-below" style="border-top:1px solid #aaa;border-bottom:1px solid #aaa;">
<span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:046CupolaSPietro.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/046CupolaSPietro.jpg/32px-046CupolaSPietro.jpg" decoding="async" width="32" height="24" class="mw-file-element" href="/wiki/Portal:Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Portal:Catholicism">Catholicism&#32;portal</a></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Catholic_social_teaching" title="Template:Catholic social teaching"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Catholic_social_teaching" title="Template talk:Catholic social teaching"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Catholic_social_teaching&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>In 2011, newly uncovered documents went up for auction and contained, among other items, proof that beginning in September 1950, while then serving as deputy of foreign affairs for the Vatican, Montini worked with former Nazis and members of the Spanish military in planning for a mercenary style army to operate within the African continent.  Another revelation was a letter from the priest of former Nazi Lieutenant Colonel <a href="/wiki/Otto_Skorzeny" title="Otto Skorzeny">Otto Skorzeny</a> to Montini in which the priest praised Montini's efforts to fund, harbour, and give safe passage to <a href="/wiki/Ratlines_(World_War_II_aftermath)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ratlines (World War II aftermath)">former Nazis evading Allied</a> capture and punishment.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The pontificate of Paul VI continued the opening and internationalisation of the church started under <a href="/wiki/Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII">Pius XII</a>. He implemented the reforms of <a href="/wiki/John_XXIII" class="mw-redirect" title="John XXIII">John XXIII</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vatican_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican II">Vatican II</a>. Yet, unlike these popes, Paul VI faced criticism throughout his papacy from both traditionalists and liberals for steering a middle course during Vatican II and during the implementation of its reforms thereafter.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGraham198375_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham198375-203">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> He expressed a desire for peace during the <a href="/wiki/Vietnam_War" title="Vietnam War">Vietnam War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On basic Church teachings, the pope was unwavering. On the tenth anniversary of <i><a href="/wiki/Humanae_vitae" title="Humanae vitae">Humanae vitae</a></i>, he reconfirmed this teaching.<sup id="cite_ref-Graham_205-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Graham-205">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> In his style and methodology, he was a disciple of Pius XII, whom he deeply revered.<sup id="cite_ref-Graham_205-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Graham-205">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> He suffered for the attacks on Pius XII for his alleged silences during the Holocaust.<sup id="cite_ref-Graham_205-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Graham-205">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Pope Paul VI was said to have been less intellectually gifted than his predecessors: he was not credited with an encyclopaedic memory, nor a gift for languages, nor the brilliant writing style of Pius XII,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPallenberg1960107_206-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPallenberg1960107-206">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> nor did he have the charisma and outpouring love, sense of humor and human warmth of John XXIII. He took on himself the unfinished reform work of these two popes, bringing them diligently with great humility and common sense and without much fanfare to conclusion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGraham198376_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham198376-207">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> In doing so, Paul VI saw himself following in the footsteps of the Apostle Paul, who, being torn to several directions, said, "I am attracted to two sides at once, because the Cross always divides."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967159_208-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuitton1967159-208">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paul6statue.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/38/Paul6statue.jpg/160px-Paul6statue.jpg" decoding="async" width="160" height="234" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/38/Paul6statue.jpg/240px-Paul6statue.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/38/Paul6statue.jpg/320px-Paul6statue.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1249" data-file-height="1825" /></a><figcaption>A statue of Paul VI in Milan, Italy</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GER_Bundesverdienstkreuz_7_Grosskreuz.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/GER_Bundesverdienstkreuz_7_Grosskreuz.svg/160px-GER_Bundesverdienstkreuz_7_Grosskreuz.svg.png" decoding="async" width="160" height="44" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/GER_Bundesverdienstkreuz_7_Grosskreuz.svg/240px-GER_Bundesverdienstkreuz_7_Grosskreuz.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/GER_Bundesverdienstkreuz_7_Grosskreuz.svg/320px-GER_Bundesverdienstkreuz_7_Grosskreuz.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="218" data-file-height="60" /></a><figcaption>Paul VI received the Grand Cross First Class of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Merit_of_the_Federal_Republic_of_Germany" title="Order of Merit of the Federal Republic of Germany">Order of Merit of the Federal Republic of Germany</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Paul VI refused to excommunicate opponents. He admonished but did not punish those with other views. The new theological freedoms which he fostered&#160;resulted in a pluralism of opinions and uncertainties among the faithful.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1991389_209-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1991389-209">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> New demands were voiced, which were taboo at the council: the reintegration of divorced Catholics, the sacramental character of the confession, and the role of women in the church and its ministries. Conservatives complained "women wanted to be priests, priests wanted to get married, bishops became regional popes and theologians claimed absolute teaching authority. Protestants claimed equality, homosexuals and the divorced called for full acceptance."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMartin1981277_210-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMartin1981277-210">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> Changes such as the <a href="/wiki/Mass_of_Paul_VI#Liturgical_orientation" title="Mass of Paul VI">reorientation of the liturgy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mass_of_Paul_VI#Changes_in_the_Order_of_Mass" title="Mass of Paul VI">alterations to the ordinary of the Mass</a>, alterations to the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">liturgical calendar</a> in the motu proprio <i><a href="/wiki/Mysterii_Paschalis" title="Mysterii Paschalis">Mysterii Paschalis</a></i>, and the <a href="/wiki/Mass_of_Paul_VI#Repositioning_of_the_tabernacle" title="Mass of Paul VI">relocation of the tabernacle</a> were controversial among some Catholics.
</p><p>While the total number of Catholics increased during the pontificate of Paul VI, the number of priests did not keep up. In the United States, at beginning of Paul's reign there were almost 1,600 priestly ordinations a year, while the number dropped to nearly 900 a year at his death. The number of seminarians at the same time dropped by three quarters. More pronounced declines were evident in religious life where the number of sisters and brothers declined sharply. Infant baptisms began to decline almost at once after Paul's election and did not begin to recover until 1980. In the same period adult conversions to the church declined by a third. While marriages increased <a href="/wiki/Declaration_of_nullity" title="Declaration of nullity">annulments</a> also increased but at a much greater rate. There was a 1,322% increase in declarations of nullity between 1968 and 1970 alone. While 65% of US Catholics went to Sunday Mass in 1965, that percentage had slipped to 40% by the time of Paul's death. Similar collapses occurred in other developed countries.<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Paul VI did renounce many traditional symbols of the papacy and the Catholic Church; some of his changes to the papal dress were reversed by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> in the early 21st century. Refusing a Vatican army of colourful military uniforms from past centuries, he got rid of them. He became the first pope to visit five continents.<sup id="cite_ref-Josef_Schmitz_van_Vorst,_68_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Josef_Schmitz_van_Vorst,_68-212">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> Paul VI systematically continued and completed the efforts of his predecessors, to turn the Euro-centric church into a church of the world, by integrating the bishops from all continents in its government and in the Synods which he convened. His 6 August 1967 <a href="/wiki/Motu_proprio" title="Motu proprio">motu proprio</a> <i>Pro Comperto Sane</i> opened the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a> to the bishops of the world. Until then, only Cardinals could be leading members of the Curia.<sup id="cite_ref-Josef_Schmitz_van_Vorst,_68_212-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Josef_Schmitz_van_Vorst,_68-212">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some critiqued Paul VI's decision; the newly created Synod of Bishops had an advisory role only and could not make decisions on their own, although the Council decided exactly that. During the pontificate of Paul VI, five such synods took place, and he is on record of implementing all their decisions.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> Related questions were raised about the new National Bishop Conferences, which became mandatory after Vatican II. Others questioned his Ostpolitik and contacts with Communism and the deals he engaged in for the faithful.<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The pope clearly suffered from the responses within the church to <i>Humanae vitae</i>. Most regions and bishops supported the pontiff, including notable support from <a href="/wiki/Patrick_O%27Boyle_(American_bishop)" class="mw-redirect" title="Patrick O&#39;Boyle (American bishop)">Patrick O'Boyle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> However, a small part of the church, especially in the Netherlands, Canada, and Germany openly disagreed with the pope, which deeply wounded him for the rest of his life.<sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<li id="cite_note-59"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-59">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In theory any male Catholic is eligible for election to the papacy. In fact, his photograph was published in <i><a href="/wiki/Life_(magazine)" title="Life (magazine)">Life</a></i> magazine with the other potential candidates for the papacy in 1958. However, the cardinals in modern times almost always elect a fellow cardinal to the office.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-74"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-74">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">28 October 1965.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-107"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-107">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">As a gesture of goodwill, the pope gave to the UN two pieces of papal jewellery, a diamond cross<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> and ring,<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> with the hopes that the proceeds from their sale at auction would contribute to the UN's efforts to end human suffering.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-143"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-143">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"> And John XXIII.</span>

<li id="cite_note-7"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-7">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20160604135530/http://www.diocesi.brescia.it/diocesi/varie/documenti/lettera_del_Vescovo_per_anno_montiniano_2014_2015.pdf">"Letter to the diocese for calling a "Montinian Year" (in Italian)"</a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span>.
